# Evolution Roadmap 1.1.31 to 2.0.0

This roadmap is the planning chain for every devthink release between 1.1.31 and 2.0.0. Each release carries exactly one hundred changes. Each change is one concrete verifiable step, correlated changes stay grouped by context inside each release, and every release builds on the previous one, forming a single production chain from the 1.1.31 baseline to the 2.0.0 platform. The feature grounding comes from the crx feature mining evidence in docs/12.crxfeaturemining.md and from the architecture skill: library first, consent first, no arbitrary limits, no hardcoded infrastructure.

## Chain rules

| Rule | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| Release cadence | one release at a time, fully validated, pushed and released before the next begins |
| Change budget | each release carries exactly one hundred planned changes, never more |
| Grouping | correlated changes stay grouped by context inside each release list |
| Grounding | every change traces to the feature pool in docs/12.crxfeaturemining.md or to the architecture skill |
| Consent | capability growth never removes the consent-first gates, it removes arbitrary limits only |
| Naming | lowercase identifiers, no underscores, no hyphens, english docs |
| Limits | the agent capability set is unbounded; every bound in code must be a user choice, never a hard cap |

## Phase map

| Phase | Versions | Theme |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 1.1.32 to 1.1.39 | unlimited agentic core |
| 2 | 1.1.40 to 1.1.49 | perception, media and network |
| 3 | 1.1.50 to 1.1.59 | workflow, automation and agent protocol |
| 4 | 1.1.60 to 1.1.69 | execution environments, security and interface |
| 5 | 1.1.70 to 1.1.79 | resilience, data and multi agent scale |
| 6 | 1.1.80 to 1.1.89 | integration, distribution and library modes |
| 7 | 1.1.90 to 2.0.0-rc.2 | consolidation and release candidates |
| 8 | 2.0.0 | the agentic browser platform |

## 1.1.32 unlimited agentic core

The first release of the chain removes every arbitrary bound in the library and grows the action vocabulary from eleven kinds to sixty three kinds. The consent gates stay: sessions, plan review and origin matching remain the boundary. What disappears is every fixed ceiling that limited how much an agent could see, wait, plan or do.

Changes:

1. wait duration accepts any positive millisecond value with no upper bound in policy
2. page bridge wait honors the full reviewed duration
3. plan proposals accept an unlimited number of steps
4. audit trail retention becomes configurable with an unlimited default
5. snapshot captures every interactive element on the page
6. snapshot captures every form control on the page
7. snapshot lists every option of each select element
8. snapshot text preview carries the complete page text
9. element labels keep their full text in observations
10. extract returns the complete text of the reviewed target
11. link extraction covers every link on the page
12. plan expiry window becomes configurable without a fixed ceiling
13. action kind presskey dispatches keydown, keypress and keyup with key and modifiers
14. action kind clickdeep runs the full pointer sequence mousedown, pointerup and click
15. action kind rightclick fires contextmenu events
16. action kind doubleclick fires dblclick sequences
17. action kind drag starts an html5 drag with a data transfer payload
18. action kind drop completes an html5 drop on the reviewed target
19. action kind upload sets reviewed files on a file input through a data transfer
20. action kind clear empties a field with input and change events
21. action kind check marks a checkbox with proper events
22. action kind uncheck clears a checkbox with proper events
23. action kind toggle flips the checked state of a checkbox
24. action kind submit submits the owning form of the target
25. action kind reload reloads the current page
26. action kind back navigates the history stack backwards
27. action kind forward navigates the history stack forwards
28. action kind scrollpage scrolls the window by reviewed amounts
29. action kind scrollby scrolls a scrollable container by reviewed amounts
30. action kind scrollend scrolls the window to the page end
31. action kind scrolltop scrolls the window to the page top
32. action kind readattribute returns any attribute of the target
33. action kind readstyle returns computed styles of the target
34. action kind readgeometry returns the bounding rectangle of the target
35. action kind readvalue returns the current value of a form control
36. action kind readtext returns the full inner text of the target
37. action kind readhtml returns the outer html of the target
38. action kind countelements counts matches of a selector
39. action kind readtable returns a table as structured rows and headers
40. action kind readlinks returns every link with anchor text and href
41. action kind readimages returns image sources with alt text
42. action kind readmeta returns the meta tags of the page
43. action kind readforms returns the state of every form control
44. action kind readstorage reads local storage entries
45. action kind writestorage writes a local storage entry
46. action kind waitfor waits until a selector appears on the page
47. action kind waittext waits until text appears on the page
48. action kind highlight outlines a target without side effects
49. action kind setattribute sets an attribute on the target
50. action kind removeattribute removes an attribute from the target
51. action kind evaluate runs reviewed javascript in the page and returns the result
52. action kind fullscreen toggles the document fullscreen state
53. action kind tablist lists every open tab with url and title
54. action kind tabcreate opens a new tab with a reviewed url
55. action kind tabactivate activates a tab by id
56. action kind tabclose closes a tab by id
57. action kind tabreload reloads a tab by id
58. action kind tabsnapshot captures the visible area of the active tab
59. action kind windowlist lists every open window
60. action kind windowcreate opens a new window with reviewed bounds
61. action kind windowclose closes a window by id
62. action kind zoomset sets the zoom of a tab
63. action kind windowresize resizes a window by id
64. action kind downloadfile downloads a reviewed url to disk
65. manifest declares optional permissions for tabs, downloads, clipboardread and clipboardwrite
66. cli manifest validator allows the optional set while keeping required permissions locked
67. background negotiates available capabilities through the permissions api
68. context payload reports the available capability set
69. capability gate refuses browser kinds when their permission is absent
70. sidepanel requests a capability grant when a step needs it

## 1.1.33 interaction universe completion

The 1.1.32 release left the library with sixty three action kinds and no arbitrary limits, and 1.1.33 completes the interaction universe on top of that core. Pointer paths, viewport coordinate clicks, text and aria resolution, timed typing, key holds, sliders, dates, colors, shadow dom piercing and iframe entry join the vocabulary, while retry rules absorb pages that move under the pointer. Every new kind passes the same session, plan review and origin gates that 1.1.32 established.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains targetref with selector, text, aria, name, xpath, index and point modes
2. types.ts gains pointpath with start point, end point, waypoints and duration
3. types.ts gains speedprofile with easing shape, peak velocity and a jitter window
4. types.ts gains keyholdstate with key, modifiers and a hold id
5. types.ts gains dialogpolicy with accept, dismiss and prompt answer rules
6. types.ts gains framepath that addresses targets inside same origin iframes
7. types.ts gains shadowpath that addresses targets across open shadow roots
8. types.ts gains retryrule with attempts, settle window and movement tolerance
9. types.ts gains clickablemap with numbered entries, labels and target modes
10. policy validates every targetref mode and rejects empty or ambiguous resolutions
11. policy grades clickpoint, shiftclick, dismissdialog and enterframe as sensitive kinds
12. policy requires a reviewed prompt answer before any dialogpolicy runs
13. policy keeps retryrule attempts a reviewed choice with no hardcoded ceiling
14. policy restricts pierceshadow and enterframe to the session origin grants
15. policy marks mapclicks, verifyvisible, verifyenabled and resolvexpath as read only
16. protocol documents the options grammar for targetref, pointpath and speedprofile
17. protocol envelope gains resolvedtarget with the matched element summary for review
18. protocol envelope carries clickablemap payloads with numbered entries
19. protocol context reports currently held keys per tab
20. protocol step details report the target mode used by each resolution
21. action kind movepointer travels a pointpath between two points with dispatched pointermove events
22. movepointer honors speedprofile easing and jitter for realistic pointer speeds
23. action kind clickpoint clicks at reviewed viewport coordinates with a full pointer sequence
24. action kind shiftclick clicks with the shift modifier for range and multi selection
25. action kind clicktext resolves the element by visible text and clicks it
26. action kind clickaria resolves the element by aria role and name pair and clicks it
27. action kind clickname resolves the element by accessible name and clicks it
28. action kind resolvexpath resolves an xpath expression and returns element details without side effects
29. action kind typetime types with a per keystroke delay for slow controls
30. action kind appendtext appends reviewed text to the current field value
31. action kind setvalue sets a form control value through the dom property with input and change events
32. action kind typeedit types into content editable regions with beforeinput and input events
33. action kind keyhold presses a key down and keeps it held across steps under a hold id
34. action kind keyrelease releases a held key by hold id
35. action kind submitsearch presses enter inside a search box and waits for the results region
36. action kind selectmulti chooses several options in a multi select control
37. action kind chooseradio picks one option of a radio group by value or label
38. action kind setslider drags a range slider to a reviewed value
39. action kind setdate sets a date input with change events
40. action kind setcolor sets a color input with change events
41. action kind expanddetails opens a collapsed details or accordion section
42. action kind dismissdialog answers an unexpected dialog and unblocks the page
43. action kind pierceshadow interacts with an element inside an open shadow root
44. action kind enterframe routes a step inside a same origin iframe
45. action kind retryaction reruns a failed interaction when the element moved during dispatch
46. action kind mapclicks returns a numbered map of every clickable element with texts and modes
47. action kind verifyvisible checks that the target is rendered and reports its geometry
48. action kind verifyenabled checks that the target is enabled and not readonly
49. page bridge resolution engine resolves targetref modes against the live dom in one pass
50. page bridge resolution reports ambiguous text matches with candidate lists
51. page bridge movement math interpolates waypoints with the reviewed easing shape
52. page bridge dispatches pointerover, pointermove and pointerout around pointer paths
53. page bridge settles each pointer hop before dispatching the next one
54. page bridge pierces open shadow roots recursively until it finds the reviewed target
55. page bridge walks the framepath through same origin iframes and refuses cross origin hops
56. page bridge retry loop revalidates the target geometry after each attempt
57. page bridge dialog handler applies the reviewed dialogpolicy before any step dispatch
58. page bridge builds the clickablemap with stable numbers inside one observation version
59. page bridge scrolls targets into view before any pointer interaction
60. page bridge focuses the target before typing when the reviewed option asks for it
61. background keeps the keyholdstate registry per tab across service worker restarts
62. background registers the dialog auto handler for tabs with an active session
63. background routes the new kinds through the existing capability gates
64. background attaches resolvedtarget summaries to every step result
65. background audits dialog decisions with the reviewed answer and the dialog text
66. background audits held keys with press and release timestamps
67. background enforces retryrule bounds from the reviewed plan instead of code constants
68. background reports held keys in the context payload refreshed per step
69. memory stores every clickablemap with its observation version
70. memory accessor getmap returns a stored clickablemap by observation version
71. memory stores keyholdstate entries with tab and step provenance
72. memory stores dialog decisions for the audit trail
73. memory stores retry outcomes with attempts and movement deltas
74. memory stores resolution summaries per target mode for later selector derivation
75. progress records verifyvisible and verifyenabled outcomes as step evidence
76. audit kinds grow with pointer, dialog, hold and retry events
77. sidepanel renders the clickablemap as a numbered list beside the plan
78. sidepanel lets the user pick a numbered map entry as the next step target
79. sidepanel shows resolvedtarget details on each interaction step before approval

84. sidepanel marks retryaction attempts on the step timeline
85. vitest policy tests cover every new kind, its risk class and its options validation

## 1.1.34 observation depth

The interaction universe of 1.1.33 gave the agent every way to act, and 1.1.34 gives it every way to see. The accessibility tree, reader views, list and table detection, embedded json state, mutation watching, network quiet waits, banner, virtualization, lazy image, sticky and scroll lock detection join the observation vocabulary, with snapshot diffing, selection reading and stable selector derivation closing the loop. Every observer stays read only behind the same gates that 1.1.33 enforced for interactions.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains observationmode with passive, watching and diffing classes
2. types.ts gains a11ynode with role, name, states, value and child refs
3. types.ts gains readerarticle with title, byline, blocks and text stats
4. types.ts gains listpattern with item selector, repeat count and sample fields
5. types.ts gains tableshape with header row, column specs and caption
6. types.ts gains jsonstate with script url, root path and parsed payload
7. types.ts gains mutationwatch with selector scopes, event kinds and a lifetime window
8. types.ts gains quietrule with network idle threshold, poll interval and timeout
9. types.ts grows the observation schema with a11y, reader, listpattern, tableshape and diff sections
10. policy grades every new observation kind as read only with no page mutation
11. policy requires a reviewed lifetime window for every watch kind
12. policy keeps the quietrule threshold and timeout user configured without hardcoded caps
13. policy gates readjson payload inspection behind the session origin grants
14. protocol observation envelope gains a11y, reader, listpattern and tableshape sections
15. protocol envelope gains mutationevent records with timestamps and target paths
16. protocol envelope gains snapshotdiff with added, removed and changed nodes
17. protocol reports the detected page language and template class in the context payload
18. protocol derives selector candidates with stability scores in element details
19. action kind a11ytree captures the accessibility tree with roles, names and states
20. action kind readvisible returns the rendered text of each element excluding hidden nodes
21. action kind readertree extracts the article reader view with title and blocks
22. action kind detectlists finds repeated item lists with a shared item selector
23. action kind detecttables finds data tables and lists their headers and shapes
24. action kind readjson extracts embedded json state from inline scripts
25. action kind watchmutate observes dom mutations inside reviewed selector scopes
26. action kind waitquiet waits until the network stays quiet for the reviewed threshold
27. action kind watchbanner watches for cookie and consent banners and reports their controls
28. action kind detectinfinitescroll detects infinite scroll containers and their triggers
29. action kind detectvirtual detects virtualized lists that render rows on demand
30. action kind detectlazy detects lazy loaded images and placeholder states
31. action kind readscrollpos returns the scroll position of the window and containers
32. action kind readlang returns the page language from the document and content signals
33. action kind readoutline returns the title and headings outline of the page
34. action kind countpages counts pagination results and estimates total pages
35. action kind listshadow enumerates open shadow roots and their host paths
36. action kind listframes enumerates iframes with their origins and sizes
37. action kind classifypage classifies the page by its detected template
38. action kind fingerprintsection computes a stable fingerprint of a page section

41. action kind watchfocus observes focus changes across the page

43. action kind detectscrolllock detects scroll locks and modal states

45. action kind detectlanguage detects the language of extracted text and routes it
46. action kind deriveselector derives a stable selector for a reviewed element
47. page bridge walker pierces shadow roots and same origin iframes while building the a11y tree

52. page bridge mutation observer batches records and throttles dispatch to the bridge
53. page bridge quiet probe samples in flight requests through the performance timeline
54. page bridge banner detector matches consent banner shapes against known patterns
55. page bridge scroll detector measures scroll ranges to flag infinite scroll containers

57. page bridge lazy detector inspects loading attributes and intersection observers

## 1.1.35 navigation mastery

With 1.1.34 the agent could see the whole page, and 1.1.35 makes it move anywhere. New window and private navigation, cache bypass reloads, load and url pattern waits, link following, single page app routing, query rewriting, per site wait profiles, redirect and error detection, auth handling, printing, prefetching and per domain rate limits join the vocabulary. The consent gates stay exactly where 1.1.34 left them: every navigation remains a reviewed step inside the session origin grants.

1. types.ts gains navtarget with url, container, position and private flags

## 1.1.36 tabs and windows command

The navigation mastery of 1.1.35 moved the agent through pages, and 1.1.36 gives it command over the whole browser surface. Tab queries, duplication, pattern closes, pins, mutes, moves, groups, discards, zoom steps, watchers, window states, layouts, clone detection and badges turn tabs and windows into first class plan targets. Limits stay user choices, never hardcoded caps, and every close still asks for consent as the chain has required since 1.1.32.

## 1.1.37 forms and data part one

With 1.1.36 the agent commanded tabs and windows, and 1.1.37 turns to structured form work. Field detection, label and placeholder matching, realistic value generation, saved profiles, wizard and dependent control handling, typeahead and calendar picking, error reading and submission retries join the vocabulary. Passwords, payments and submissions stay behind explicit consent, while honeypots, captchas and login or checkout templates are detected and routed to the user instead of being forced.

## 1.1.38 forms and data part two

The form vocabulary of 1.1.37 could fill and submit, and 1.1.38 turns filled pages into structured data. Table scraping with header normalization, span handling and nested tables joins csv, json, excel, clipboard and sheet exports, csv import for fill loops, row iteration, transforms, deduplication, pagination, multi page merges, timestamping, grid previews, disk streaming, resume and provenance logging. Extraction stays auditable end to end: every row keeps its source url, timestamp and step reference.

## 1.1.39 files clipboard and downloads

The extraction pipeline of 1.1.38 produced data, and 1.1.39 closes phase one by moving bytes safely. Batch downloads with pause, resume and verification, mime interception, network log export, consented clipboard reads and writes, screenshot copying, quarantine, virus scanning hooks, consistent capture naming and artifact cleanup complete the file story. With the unlimited agentic core finished from 1.1.32 through 1.1.39, phase two can build perception and media on a complete foundation.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains downloadspec with urls, filename rule and completion criteria

## 1.1.40 media capture part one

The unlimited core closed with 1.1.39 and phase two opens by giving the agent eyes of its own. Viewport, full page, element and region capture arrive with pixel ratio scaling, step marker annotations, element contact sheets, before and after state pairs around every action and consistent export naming. Every capture stays read only evidence behind the session, plan review and origin gates that 1.1.32 established.

## 1.1.41 media capture part two

Where 1.1.40 captured still images, 1.1.41 widens the lens to documents and moving media. Pdf capture with custom page sizes and paginated reports joins tab screencast recording, task run video, tab audio, video frame stills, image detection and batch download, canvas and webrtc capture, media metadata, asset collection, time lapse capture, format conversion and thumbnails. Recording remains sensitive capture of user activity and always asks consent first.

## 1.1.42 network observation part one

Media capture closed with 1.1.41 and 1.1.42 turns from the page to the network. The agent gains extension side fetching with consented custom headers, timeout and retry policies, streamed large responses, json parsing into named fields, dom parsing of fetched markup and typed rest and graphql calls. Every outbound request leaves only through granted origins, reviewed plans and the audit trail.

## 1.1.43 network observation part two

The direct calls of 1.1.42 taught the agent to speak, and 1.1.43 teaches it to listen. Websocket channels arrive with reconnection backoff and message multiplexing, joined by page request and response observation, header inspection, response body capture, server sent event subscription, a long polling helper with cancellation, request correlation ids and page api discovery for extraction. Observation is read only and scoped to the run tab and its granted origins.

## 1.1.44 network control

Observation closed with 1.1.43 and 1.1.44 takes the steering wheel over traffic. Request blocking, response mocking, header rewriting, cookie control per domain, oauth flows, consented api key storage, proxy routing with task only scope, rate limit respect, form posts and multipart uploads complete the traffic story. Every rule applies for the run only and reverts the moment the run ends, fails or is cancelled.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains blockrule with url pattern, resource type and run scope
2. types.ts gains mockspec with url pattern, status, headers and body fixture
3. types.ts gains headerule with url pattern, header name, operation and value
4. types.ts gains cookierecord with name, domain, path, value and expiry
5. types.ts gains oauthflow with provider, authorize url, token url, scopes and redirect origin
6. types.ts gains tokenrecord with access token, refresh token, scopes and expiry
7. types.ts gains apikeyentry with name, origin scope, created time and last use

## 1.1.45 debugging part one

Network control closed with 1.1.44 and 1.1.45 opens the debugging chain by recording what pages say and how they misbehave. Console capture at every level, javascript errors with stack traces, unhandled rejections, failed requests, long task timings, spam pattern detection, log rotation, structured per step levels and console diffing between runs assemble into one run timeline. The timeline is read only evidence bound to step ids and consent per origin.

## 1.1.46 debugging part two

The run timeline of 1.1.45 recorded symptoms, and 1.1.46 attaches the chrome devtools protocol to find their causes. Raw cdp commands, domain event subscriptions, breakpoints, code stepping, paused dom capture, watch expressions and page script overrides arrive behind the optional debugger capability and explicit consent. Clean attach, clean detach and safe teardown hold on every path, including the user closing the debugger.

## 1.1.47 debugging part three

The protocol channel of 1.1.46 gives 1.1.47 its instruments. Flow performance measurement, on demand heap snapshots, memory growth tracking, cpu profiles, layout shift detection, devtools trace export with step annotations, offline trace replay, iframe and worker attach, page service worker debugging and source map capture close the debugging chain. Every heavy artifact respects user configured ceilings and expires by the retention window.

## 1.1.48 emulation

The debugging chain closed with 1.1.47 and 1.1.48 lets the agent wear masks. Device metric, network condition, geographic location and per task user agent emulation join permission overrides and third party script blackboxing, each as a reviewed layer with a revert plan. Emulation never leaks past the run: layers stack only when the plan lists them and revert on end, cancel, failure or tab drop.

## 1.1.49 memory part one

The perception, media and network phase closes with 1.1.49 by making every run survivable. Task state persists across service worker restarts, session history gains timestamps, full session snapshots join crash and on demand restore, interval auto snapshots, named organization, session diffing, cross session search and session import and export. Restore reopens only granted origins and every snapshot expires by user choice, never by a hardcoded cap.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains taskstate with run id, step cursor, outputs and last checkpoint time
2. types.ts gains sessionevent with kind, time, tab id and detail
3. types.ts gains sessionrecord with id, name, created time, tabs and captures
4. types.ts gains sessiontab with url, title, index, scroll position and form state
5. types.ts gains snapshotplan with scope, sections and capture flags
6. types.ts gains restoreplan with tab policy, form policy and capture policy
7. types.ts gains autointerval with period, max snapshots and expiry
8. types.ts gains sessionfolder with name, parent and tag list
9. types.ts gains sessiondiff result with tab, url and form change classes
10. types.ts gains searchquery with terms, fields and time window
11. types.ts gains sessionfile with format version, record ids and byte size
12. toolstep options carry snapshot, restore and search parameters
13. new module sessions.ts groups persistence, snapshot, diff and search logic
14. sessions exports persist, snapshot, restore, diff and search functions
15. sessions exports through the library index
16. taskstate checksums its payload for corruption detection
17. policy validates the options payload of every session kind
18. policy marks persiststate, capturesession and searchsessions as read only in the risk table
19. policy grades restoresession as sensitive because it opens tabs and writes form state
20. policy gate sessiongate requires review of every restoreplan before restore
21. policy grades importsessions as sensitive with full record review
22. policy bounds auto snapshot intervals by user choice only
23. policy bounds snapshot retention by user choice with no hard cap
24. policy validates search queries against the term grammar
25. policy requires the grant of every origin a restore reopens
26. policy grades diffsessions as read only comparison evidence
27. policy validates session names and folder names for uniqueness
28. policy keeps crash restore prompts inside the session consent model
29. policy exposes snapshot section toggles as user choices
30. policy requires export review before session files leave the device
31. policy marks namedsessions filing as read only organization
32. policy restores captures and cookies only with their origin grants
33. protocol documents the session options grammar per kind

35. protocol carries snapshot and restore ids in step result details

37. protocol parseproposal rejects import files with unknown format versions
38. protocol carries search match counts in result details
39. protocol version bumps to carry the session contract
40. action kind persiststate writes the task state of the run

62. action kind searchsessions searches across saved sessions

## 1.1.50 workflow engine part one

The perception and media phase closed with 1.1.49 and phase three opens with the workflow engine core. Steps compose into workflows, blocks nest for reuse, variables live in typed scopes and expressions bind them together, with regex extraction, jittered delays, element waits, single step execution and dry runs for safety. Every run still passes the consent gates that 1.1.32 established: session, plan review and origin matching.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains workflowrecord with id, name, version, origin grants and a step list
2. types.ts gains workflowstep with kind, target, options, bindings and a label
3. types.ts gains workflowblock that nests child steps under a reusable name
4. types.ts gains steptemplate that marks a step as shareable across workflows
5. types.ts gains variablescope with string, number, boolean, list and element kinds
6. types.ts gains variablebinding that links a step output to a named variable
7. types.ts gains expressiontype with operands, operator and result kind
8. types.ts gains regexrule with pattern, flags and named capture groups
9. types.ts gains delaystep with base milliseconds and a jitter window
10. types.ts gains waitstep with selector, timeout and poll interval
11. types.ts gains workflowrun with id, workflow id, state, start time and end time
12. types.ts gains runstate values pending, running, paused, done, failed and cancelled
13. types.ts gains runlog entry with step id, state, duration and outcome details
14. types.ts gains the dryrun flag that marks a run as free of side effects
15. new module workflow.ts groups every workflow type, engine function and executor

17. engine function expandblocks flattens nested blocks into an executable step list
18. engine function validateworkflow checks kinds, scopes and bindings before a run
19. engine function runworkflow advances a workflowrun one step at a time
20. engine function runstep executes exactly one step and returns its outcome
21. engine function bindvariables resolves all bindings before each step runs
22. engine function pushscope opens a child scope for a block invocation
23. engine function popscope closes a block scope and keeps the parent intact
24. expressioneval evaluates arithmetic, comparison and logic operators between variables
25. expressioneval coerces operand kinds and rejects mismatched operators
26. expressioneval resolves variable references from the nearest scope outward
27. regexextract applies a regexrule to text and stores named captures as variables
28. regexextract reports the no match case as a step outcome instead of a crash
29. delayjitter samples a delay inside the reviewed window with a seeded random source
30. engine checkpoints run state after every completed step
31. engine resumes a paused workflowrun from its last checkpoint
32. engine cancels a running workflowrun and records the reason
33. engine refuses to run a workflow outside an approved session
34. engine refuses steps whose target origin falls outside the session grants
35. engine refuses variable references that no scope defines
36. executor map routes every workflow step kind to its existing action implementation
37. delay executor sleeps with alarms when the delay exceeds the service worker lifetime
38. element wait executor polls a selector until appearance or timeout
39. element wait executor aborts cleanly when the tab navigates away
40. dryrun executor evaluates every step and performs no browser mutation
41. dryrun executor records the would be outcome of each step in the runlog
42. dryrun executor refuses steps whose kind has no read only projection
43. single step execution runs one chosen step outside the run loop
44. executor outcomes feed variable bindings for the following steps
45. workflow runs survive service worker restarts through checkpoint restore
46. policy validates the workflow grammar before any run is proposed
47. policy validates expression operators against operand kinds
48. policy validates regexrule patterns and rejects unbounded backtracking shapes
49. policy validates delay and wait bounds as user configured values without hard caps
50. policy marks dryrun as a read only class in the risk table
51. policy gates every workflow behind the existing plan review consent
52. policy requires block expansion before review so no step stays hidden
53. policy grades workflows that write, submit or navigate as sensitive for review
54. protocol envelope gains workflowproposal with the expanded step list
55. protocol envelope gains workflowoutcome with run id, state and step outcomes
56. protocol envelope gains stepoutcome with bindings produced and consumed
57. response envelope returns single step outcomes for debugging callers
58. protocol carries the dryrun flag through proposal and outcome envelopes

68. audit records dryrun executions with their read only outcome
69. progress tracks workflow completion as executed steps over total steps

## 1.1.51 workflow engine part two

The 1.1.50 engine composed steps, scopes and dry runs, and this release gives it control flow. Conditionals over extracted values, branching on page state, loops over lists, elements and conditions, parallel branches with joins, retries, error handlers, try catch and timeouts make workflows robust on fragile pages. Safety bounds stay user choices, never hardcoded caps.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains conditionstep that tests an expression over extracted values
2. types.ts gains branchstep that selects a path by page state
3. types.ts gains loopstep that iterates over a data list variable
4. types.ts gains repeatuntilstep that repeats a block until an expression holds
5. types.ts gains whilestep with a condition and a configurable safety bound
6. types.ts gains foreachstep that iterates over matched elements
7. types.ts gains parallelstep that runs branches concurrently
8. types.ts gains joinstep that merges parallel branch outcomes
9. types.ts gains trystep that wraps fragile steps with a catch block
10. types.ts gains retrypolicy with attempts, backoff shape and retryable error classes
11. types.ts gains errorhandler with catch steps and a rerun option
12. types.ts gains timeoutpolicy with per step and per run millisecond budgets
13. types.ts gains loopcounter that records iteration indices for audit
14. types.ts gains branchoutcome that carries the chosen path and its reason
15. types.ts gains the cancelled error class for timeout aborts
16. engine function evaluatecondition runs expressioneval over extracted values
17. engine function choosebranch inspects page state and selects the matching path
18. engine function runloop iterates a block over a list variable
19. engine function runrepeatuntil runs a block again until the condition holds
20. engine function runwhile honors the configured safety bound and reports overflow
21. engine function runforeach resolves matched elements and iterates them
22. engine function runparallel launches branches concurrently with per branch contexts
23. engine function joinbranches waits for all branches and merges their outcomes
24. engine function runtry executes a fragile block and catches failures
25. engine function runcatch executes error handler steps on failure
26. engine function applyretry retries a failed step under the retrypolicy
27. engine function applytimeout aborts a step that exceeds its budget
28. engine function applyruntimeout aborts a whole run that exceeds its budget
29. engine records the failing step, its error class and the handler path in the runlog
30. parallel branches receive isolated variable scopes that merge at the join
31. loop iterations receive the iteration index and current item as variables
32. while and repeatuntil emit iteration markers into the runlog
33. join resolves conflicting variable writes with the last branch winning by default
34. join supports reviewed merge strategies first, last and fail on conflict
35. timeout aborts cancel in flight page actions through the executor seams
36. retry backoff accepts fixed and exponential shapes with jitter
37. branch steps default to an else path when no condition matches
38. loops deep copy the item variable per iteration to avoid cross iteration mutation
39. nested loops track an outer index and inner index path for audit
40. parallel branch count stays a user choice with no engine cap
41. join policy decides whether sibling branches cancel or continue on branch failure
42. condition executor reuses expressioneval with no page side effects
43. branch executor reads page state through the snapshot accessor
44. loop executor binds the current item and index before each iteration
45. parallel executor schedules branches through the background task scheduler
46. try executor captures the error class and message for the handler
47. retry executor reruns the underlying action kind with fresh bindings
48. timeout executor races the step against an alarm
49. policy validates branch paths as unique and terminating
50. policy validates loop safety bounds as user configured with a documented default
51. policy validates retrypolicy attempts as user configured with no hard cap
52. policy grades retryable write steps as sensitive and keeps them behind review
53. policy requires an else path for every branchstep
54. policy rejects while loops without a safety bound
55. policy grades parallel branches that navigate as needing per branch review
56. policy validates timeout budgets as positive user configured values

58. workflowoutcome gains branch outcomes and loop counters
59. protocol documents the control flow grammar and merge strategies
60. response envelope reports timeout and retry exhaustion distinctly

## 1.1.52 triggers and scheduling

Workflows gained control flow in 1.1.51 and now they start themselves. Page visits, url patterns, the context menu, keyboard shortcuts, the toolbar button, cron schedules, intervals, url lists, webhooks and page events become trigger rules that launch reviewed workflows. Manual runs keep a step preview so a human always sees what a trigger will do before it runs.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains triggerule with kind, match pattern and workflow reference
2. types.ts gains visitrule that fires on navigation to matching origins
3. types.ts gains urlrule with glob patterns for trigger matching
4. types.ts gains menurule that binds a workflow to a context menu entry
5. types.ts gains keyrule that binds a workflow to a keyboard shortcut
6. types.ts gains buttonrule that binds a workflow to the toolbar button
7. types.ts gains cronrule with a cron expression and timezone

9. types.ts gains urllistrule that runs a workflow across a list of urls

13. types.ts gains triggerstate with enabled, cooldown and last fire time
14. types.ts gains manualrun request with the step preview flag
15. types.ts gains rulestats with fires, launches and suppressions per rule

17. new module trigger.ts groups every trigger rule, scheduler and matcher

## 1.1.53 workflow editor interface

Triggers made workflows start themselves in 1.1.52 and this release gives them a visible home. The editor brings a canvas with drag and drop, a block palette, a mini map and a step library by category, plus run logs, run history, import and export, template sharing, local versioning with diffs, background runs, a stuck run watchdog, nested parameters and per site policy overrides. Everything the engine and trigger layers already do becomes editable end to end.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains editormodel with nodes, edges and layout state for the canvas
2. types.ts gains palettenode descriptors for every block category

11. types.ts gains watchdogconfig with stall threshold and recovery action
12. types.ts gains breakpoint marker on any step for editor debugging

## 1.1.54 agent protocol part one

The workflow stack has run from engine to editor since 1.1.53, and phase three now turns the browser into a tool server. This release exposes browser tools over mcp, lists them with json schema inputs, negotiates capabilities with clients, frames messages in json rpc, bridges stdio for local clients and binds localhost by default. Every tool call lands behind the same consent gates that govern the panels.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains tooldef with name, version, description and json schema inputs
2. types.ts gains toolcatalog with domains, versions and consent metadata
3. types.ts gains jsonrpcframe with id, method, params and result or error
4. types.ts gains capabilityset offered to clients during negotiation
5. types.ts gains clientrecord with id, transport, capabilities and pairing state
6. types.ts gains toolnamespace that prefixes tool names by domain
7. types.ts gains toolresult with content, structured payload and is error flag
8. types.ts gains transportkind values stdio and http
9. types.ts gains mcpserverconfig with bind address, port and allowed transports
10. types.ts gains rpcerror codes parse, method, params, internal and consentrefused
11. types.ts gains methodentry for the server routing table
12. types.ts gains serverstate values stopped, running and idle
13. new module mcpserver.ts groups the server, transports and frame routing
14. new module toolcatalog.ts groups tool definitions, versions and schemas
15. server function initialize completes the mcp handshake and returns server info
16. server function ping answers keepalive frames with pong
17. server function listtools returns every tool with its json schema inputs
18. server function negotiate exchanges capability sets with the client
19. server function handleframe parses and routes a jsonrpcframe
20. server function respond builds a result or error frame with the same id
21. server function dispatch invokes a tool behind the consent gates
22. server function bindlocalhost binds the http listener to localhost only
23. stdiobridge relays frames between a local client process and the server
24. stdiobridge launches through the native messaging host manifest
25. stdiobridge restarts a dead client process on demand
26. json rpc framing supports newline delimited and http post envelopes
27. frame validation rejects malformed ids, unknown methods and bad params
28. capability negotiation declares protocol version, tools and transports
29. server keeps one clientrecord per connected transport
30. server assigns tool namespaces by domain: browser, workflow, memory and system
31. tool versioning appends the version to every tooldef for compatibility checks
32. server function enqueuerequest serializes concurrent frames per client
33. server routing table maps methods to handlers through methodentry records
34. frame size and queue depth stay user configured with no hardcoded caps
35. server emits framed logs for local debugging without leaking payloads
36. browser tools expose snapshot, click, type and presskey as mcp tools
37. browser tools expose navigation, tab and window kinds as mcp tools
38. browser tools expose extraction kinds as read only mcp tools
39. workflow tools expose list, run and dryrun as mcp tools
40. workflow tools expose trigger listing so clients can inspect schedules
41. memory tools expose read accessors for runs, variables and audit summaries
42. system tools expose status, version and capability reports
43. every tooldef maps its options to a json schema with typed properties
44. tool schemas mark required properties and default values
45. tool descriptions state the consent class and side effects in plain language
46. sensitive tools declare consentmeta with the review requirement
47. tool dispatch requires an approved session before execution
48. tool dispatch refuses origins outside session grants
49. read only tools run under the dryrun risk class without extra approval
50. policy validates tool schemas against the action kind grammar
51. policy grades every tooldef with the risk class of its action kind
52. policy requires consentmeta on tools with side effects
53. policy keeps the localhost bind as the default and grades remote binding sensitive
54. policy refuses tool versions below the negotiated compatibility floor
55. policy requires explicit user enablement before the server starts
56. policy validates namespace membership so tools stay in their domain
57. protocol documents the mcp tool list and schema grammar
58. protocol envelope gains toolcall and toolresult frames
59. protocol documents capability negotiation fields
60. protocol documents stdio frame delimiting and the native host contract
61. response envelope carries tool errors with the json rpc error codes
62. protocol documents the browser, workflow, memory and system namespaces
63. memory stores clientrecord entries with pairing state
64. memory stores the negotiated capability set per client
65. memory stores stdiobridge launch events with process ids
66. memory accessor listclients returns connected client records
67. audit records tool list, negotiate and connect events
68. audit records every tool call with client id, tool name and origin

74. sidepanel starts and stops the mcp server on demand
75. sidepanel shows bridge process status with restart

## 1.1.55 agent protocol part two

The 1.1.54 server spoke stdio to local clients and this release opens the stream http transport for remote ones. Per tool consent metadata, human approval gates on sensitive tools, session tokens for pairing, tool namespacing, tool versioning, client allowlists, tls and an auth handshake make the remote surface safe. Nothing runs without a paired, allowed and authenticated client.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains httpstream transport config with tls and endpoint settings
2. types.ts gains sessiontoken with client id, issue time, expiry and scopes
3. types.ts gains pairingcode for one time client pairing
4. types.ts gains allowlistentry with client identity and granted tool namespaces
5. types.ts gains approvalrequest with tool call, reason and pending state
6. types.ts gains approvalexec record with decision, actor and timestamp
7. types.ts gains tlsconfig with certificate requirements
8. types.ts gains authchallenge with method and nonce
9. types.ts gains consentmeta fields: risk class, approval requirement and origin scope
10. types.ts gains toolversion policy floor per client
11. types.ts gains remoteconfig with endpoint, tls mode and max clients
12. types.ts gains tokenhash format for stored session tokens
13. types.ts gains approvaltimeout with the default refusal window
14. types.ts gains clientidentity with fingerprint and display name
15. sensitive tool list derives from policy grading into consentmeta
16. httpstream transport serves the json rpc endpoint for remote clients
17. httpstream transport keeps server sent event channels open for streams
18. httpstream transport heartbeats idle channels and closes dead ones
19. server function issuepairingcode creates a one time pairing code
20. server function pairclient exchanges a pairing code for a session token
21. server function verifytoken validates session tokens on every frame
22. server function checkallowlist refuses clients not on the allowlist
23. server function starttls requires valid certificates before remote traffic
24. server function issuechallenge sends an auth challenge to a new client
25. server function verifyauth completes the handshake and marks the client paired
26. server function requireapproval raises an approval gate for sensitive tools
27. server function resolveapproval executes or refuses the pending call
28. server function revokeclient invalidates a session token on demand
29. server function expiretokens enforces the configured token lifetime
30. server function enforcemaxclients refuses connections past the user configured limit
31. server function listremotestatus reports endpoint, tls and client counts
32. token scopes limit which tool namespaces a client may call
33. approval gates surface in the sidepanel with the full call arguments
34. approval gates carry the client identity and called tool in every prompt
35. approval requests time out with a configurable window and default refusal
36. server redacts call arguments in approval prompts when the user marks them secret
37. tool version negotiation picks the highest compatible version per client
38. namespace checks run before consent checks so unknown tools fail fast
39. auth failures return json rpc errors without leaking pairing state
40. tls termination happens before token verification
41. policy grades every sensitive tool with an approval gate requirement
42. policy validates allowlist entries against known client identities
43. policy validates token lifetimes as user configured values
44. policy requires tls for any non localhost transport
45. policy refuses pairing when no session is active
46. policy grades remote transport enablement as a sensitive user choice
47. policy limits token scopes to the namespaces the user granted
48. policy validates approval timeouts as user configured positive windows
49. policy grades token revocation as an always available user action
50. policy records consentmeta in every tool listing response
51. protocol documents the http stream transport and its event channels
52. protocol documents the pairing and auth handshake sequence
53. protocol documents session token scopes and expiry semantics
54. protocol documents approval gate request and response frames
55. protocol documents tls requirements for remote transports
56. protocol documents allowlist semantics and namespace scoping
57. protocol documents heartbeat and channel close semantics
58. memory stores session tokens as tokenhash values, never in the clear
59. memory stores pairing codes with their single use state
60. memory stores allowlist entries with grant history
61. memory stores approval requests and their decisions
62. memory stores auth handshake events with outcomes
63. memory stores clientidentity fingerprints for allowlist matching
64. audit records pair, revoke, expire, allow and refuse events
65. audit records approval decisions with actor and latency
66. memory accessor listapprovals returns pending and resolved gates
67. sidepanel shows remote transport status with tls state
68. sidepanel shows the pairing flow with a copyable pairing code
69. sidepanel lists paired clients with scopes and expiry
70. sidepanel revokes a client with one click

## 1.1.56 agent protocol part three

Remote clients paired through 1.1.55 now receive the full protocol. Server event subscription, resource subscriptions for page state, sampling callbacks into client models, prompts exposed as tools, streaming results, progress notifications, in flight cancellation, per client rate limiting, concurrent clients, audit logs, structured errors with retry hints, idempotency keys, batch calls, tool dry runs and tool mocks complete the agent protocol.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains eventsubscription with event kinds and filters
2. types.ts gains resourcesubscription for page state watchers
3. types.ts gains samplingrequest that asks a client model for a completion
4. types.ts gains promptdef that exposes a prompt as a callable tool
5. types.ts gains streamchunk for progressive tool results
6. types.ts gains progressnotice with percent, message and cancel hint
7. types.ts gains cancelframe that aborts an in flight tool call
8. types.ts gains ratelimit with window, budget and per client counters
9. types.ts gains structurederror with code, retry hint and retry after
10. types.ts gains idempotencykey attached to tool calls
11. types.ts gains batchcall with ordered tool calls and stop on error flag
12. types.ts gains tooldryrun flag on tool calls
13. types.ts gains toolmock that returns canned results in tests
14. types.ts gains callcontext that isolates concurrent client state
15. server function subscriberegisters a client event subscription
16. server function notifyevent pushes protocol events to subscribers
17. server function watchresource starts a page state watcher for a client
18. server function notifyresource pushes page state deltas to watchers
19. server function requestsampling sends a sampling callback to the client model
20. sampling callbacks respect the client declared model capabilities
21. server function listprompts exposes prompt defs as tools
22. server function callprompt renders a prompt and returns its arguments as a tool call
23. server function streamresults emits stream chunks as a tool runs
24. server function notifyprogress emits progress notices for long tools
25. server function canceltool aborts an in flight tool call on a cancelframe
26. cancellation propagates to the workflow engine and page executors
27. server function applyratelimit counts calls per client per window
28. rate limit excess returns a structurederror with retry after
29. server supports concurrent clients with isolated callcontext records
30. concurrent calls to the same tab serialize through the session lock
31. audit log records every tool call with idempotencykey and outcome
32. server maps executor failures to structurederror codes and retry hints
33. retry hints classify retryable timeouts, busy sessions and consent refusals
34. server function checkidempotency replays the stored result for a repeated key
35. idempotency keys expire after the configured window
36. server function runbatch executes ordered batch calls with per call results
37. batch calls stop on first error when the flag requests it
38. tool dry run evaluates arguments and consent without side effects
39. tool mocks return canned results for the declared tool names
40. mocks never touch the browser and audit records them as mocked
41. policy grades subscriptions as read only when filters exclude mutations
42. policy grades sampling callbacks as sensitive since prompts leave the browser

47. policy keeps tool dry run results free of page mutations
48. policy validates mock usage to test contexts only

## 1.1.57 llm integration

The agent protocol is complete since 1.1.56 and this release lets models drive it. Provider agnostic endpoints, model routing per task kind, local model support, openapi style tool descriptions, natural language command parsing, intent classification, goal to plan generation with review, failure replanning, per step reflection, cost and token budgets, a prompt template library and output parsing guardrails arrive together. The model proposes and the consent gates still dispose.

Changes:

10. types.ts gains costbudget with token and currency ceilings per run
11. types.ts gains usagerecord with prompt, completion and total tokens plus cost
12. types.ts gains prompttemplate with name, body, variables and version
13. types.ts gains parseguard with schema, retries and refusal markers
14. types.ts gains modeloutput with raw text, parsed payload and guard verdict

17. new module promptlibrary.ts groups templates and versions

23. llm function classifyintent maps a request to an intentkind with confidence

## 1.1.58 multi agent part one

Models have driven single agents since 1.1.57 and this release puts several agents to work at once, each in its own tab with its own role. A shared task queue with work stealing keeps every agent busy, agent mailboxes carry messages between them, and blackboard memory holds what they share. Per agent budgets, permission scopes, naming, sub agents with depth limits and a kill switch keep the swarm under consent.

Changes:

1. types.ts gains agentidentity with id, name, role and metadata
2. types.ts gains agentrole values planner, worker, observer and custom user roles
3. types.ts gains taskitem with id, lane, priority, payload and claim state
4. types.ts gains taskqueue with lanes, priorities and completion policy
5. types.ts gains claimrecord with agent id, task id and heartbeat time
6. types.ts gains agentmailbox with inbox, outbox and unread counts
7. types.ts gains agentmessage with sender, recipient, kind and payload
8. types.ts gains blackboardentry with key, value kind, author and timestamp
9. types.ts gains blackboardsection values goals, facts, findings and scratch
10. types.ts gains agentbudget with token, cost and step ceilings per agent
11. types.ts gains agentscope with the origin and tool namespaces granted
12. types.ts gains spawnrequest with role, task and depth
13. types.ts gains depthlimit that bounds sub agent recursion
14. types.ts gains killswitch state that halts every agent at once

16. new module taskqueue.ts groups queues, lanes, claims and work stealing
17. new module blackboard.ts groups shared sections and entries

35. agent function spawn creates a sub agent under a spawnrequest

## 1.1.59 multi agent part two

The 1.1.58 swarm shared a queue, a mailbox and a blackboard, and this release gives it shape. Leader worker topology, planner executor separation, critic review and verifier agents organize the work, tab handoff moves tasks between agents mid run, resource locks and conflict scans keep parallel edits safe, result merging turns parallel outputs into one report and the progressboard shows every agent at once.

## 1.1.60 execution environments

This release opens phase four on top of the agent protocol shipped in 1.1.59 by giving every reviewed step a concrete execution environment. Heavy parsing moves into offscreen documents and a pool of offscreenworker threads, injected logic stays inside an isolatedworld that page scripts cannot touch, untrusted markup renders only inside a sandboxframe, and a keepalive signal holds the service worker alive for the whole run. The consent gates do not move: every environment executes only reviewed steps against granted origins.

Changes:

1. types introduce environmentkind with values pagecontext, isolatedworld, offscreenworker and sandboxframe
2. toolstep gains an environment field naming where the step executes
3. policy validates the environment field of every step kind
4. policy marks evaluate steps as isolatedworld only by default
5. policy marks parse heavy read kinds as offscreenworker eligible
6. policy marks render steps of untrusted markup as sandboxframe only
7. types add workerrequest and workerresponse envelopes for executor traffic
8. types add sandboxrender descriptors carrying trusted markup and a nonce
9. policy refuses steps whose environment is absent from the session grant list
10. environment grants join the origin grant in the session record
11. policy exposes environment requirements per action kind
12. manifest adds the offscreen permission through the optional capability set
13. manifest declares the offscreen document with its reasons and justification
14. manifest lists the sandbox page under the sandbox key with no extension privileges
15. manifest registers content scripts in the isolated world by default
16. cli manifest validator allows the sandbox key and the offscreen declaration
17. background negotiates the offscreen capability through the permissions api
18. capability gate refuses offscreenworker steps when the grant is absent
19. audit kinds grow with environment grant, worker spawn and sandbox render events
20. observation schema reports the environment of each executed step
21. response envelope returns the environment beside the step outcome
22. protocol documents the environment grammar per kind
23. keepalive becomes a typed run state with start and stop events
24. policy limits keepalive to sessions with an active reviewed plan
25. executor registry maps each environmentkind to its runtime adapter
26. offscreenworker adapter spawns the offscreen document on first use
27. offscreenworker adapter reuses a single document across the steps of a run
28. offscreenworker adapter closes the document when the run completes
29. worker pool spawns dedicated web workers inside the offscreen document
30. worker pool grows and shrinks with the pending parse queue
31. html parsing of large snapshots moves into an offscreenworker
32. json parsing of network payloads moves into an offscreenworker
33. table row reduction moves into an offscreenworker
34. accessibility tree shaping moves into an offscreenworker
35. complex selector evaluation moves into an offscreenworker
36. screenshot stitching moves into an offscreenworker
37. workerrequest payloads move buffers as transferables where possible
38. workerresponse streams partial results back to the executor
39. executor falls back to inline parsing when the offscreen grant is absent
40. isolatedworld adapter injects step logic through the scripting api
41. isolatedworld adapter keeps page globals unreachable from step code
42. evaluate steps run inside the isolatedworld with reviewed arguments only
43. pagebridge keeps pagecontext execution for dom actions that need page events
44. pagebridge routes each step to the environment named by policy
45. sandboxframe adapter renders untrusted markup inside the sandboxed page
46. sandboxframe adapter strips scripts and event handlers before render
47. sandboxframe adapter posts render results back through postmessage
48. sandboxframe adapter assigns a per render nonce to every message
49. sandboxframe render results never reenter the dom outside the frame
50. keepalive adapter holds a runtime port open while a run is active
51. keepalive adapter closes the port when the run reaches a terminal state
52. keepalive adapter emits a heartbeat every thirty seconds of run activity
53. keepalive adapter reattaches after a service worker restart using persisted state
54. run state persists to storage on every heartbeat for restart recovery
55. executor resumes the pending step after a service worker restart
56. executor reaps zombie runs whose heartbeat stopped for three intervals
57. executor locks a session against concurrent runs through a storage level lock
58. executor serializes steps that share one tab across parallel branches
59. executor records a url history entry per navigation inside the run record
60. executor records the worker turnaround of every offloaded step
61. memory stores the environment grant list of each session
62. memory stores worker spawn and teardown events beside step outcomes
63. memory stores the heartbeat timestamps of each active run
64. memory stores the url history of each run for audit
65. memory expires stale run state past a configurable window
66. memory scopes run state per profile so parallel profiles never share it
67. memory encrypts persisted run state at rest through the storage api
68. memory tracks storage quota usage of run state and prunes on pressure
69. memory attaches provenance to every persisted environment event
70. memory exports run state for audit as a single record

72. progress records worker turnaround per step for later profiling
73. memory keeps a registry of spawned offscreen documents with their reasons
74. memory records sandbox render provenance with source origin and nonce

## 1.1.61 security part one

With execution environments in place from 1.1.60, this release hardens the trust boundary itself. Automation runs behind a per origin allowlist with per site originprofiles, a denydefault posture refuses everything ungranted, consentwindows bound every grant in time, and revokerun can halt a run mid step. Every decision lands in an immutablelog whose loghash chains each entry, while maskinputs keeps typed secrets out of every record.

Changes:

1. types introduce originprofile records with per site action kind grants
2. types introduce consentwindow records with start, duration and scope fields
3. types introduce revokerun as a first class session event
4. types introduce immutablelog as the append only run log record type
5. types introduce loghash chain fields with previous, current and algorithm values
6. types add consentscope naming origin, kinds and boundary in one grant
7. policy flips the default posture to denydefault for every ungranted origin
8. policy consults the per origin automation allowlist before any step
9. policy consults the originprofile before sensitive kinds
10. policy refuses steps on origins absent from the allowlist
11. policy treats the active tab grant as one explicit single origin grant
12. policy binds every grant to one exact origin with no wildcard expansion
13. policy scopes each consentwindow to one session and one origin
14. policy expires the consentwindow at its duration boundary
15. policy suspends the run when its consentwindow expires mid step
16. policy accepts mid run revocation as a terminal session event
17. policy halts the pending step and every queued step on revokerun
18. policy classifies sensitive kinds into payment, credential, delete and publish classes
19. policy refines sensitive classification by kind options, not kind names alone
20. policy classifies upload, download and evaluate as sensitive by default
21. policy classifies credential bearing form submits as credential class sensitive
22. policy requires a fresh consent prompt for each sensitive class per origin
23. maskinputs covers typed values, form values and stored values in logs
24. maskinputs recognizes password, token, card and secret field shapes
25. policy excludes masked values from every export of the run record
26. immutablelog accepts appends with no update or delete path
27. loghash chains every entry to the hash of its predecessor
28. loghash writes the entry hash at append time with no later rewrite
29. immutablelog seals the run log at completion with a final hash
30. policy validates the hash chain at read time and reports tamper evidence
31. executor checks the origin allowlist before dispatching each step
32. executor checks the consentwindow state before dispatching each step
33. executor routes sensitive steps through the consent prompt gate
34. consent prompt shows the exact origin, kind and class of the pending step
35. consent prompt shows the consentwindow duration as a user choice
36. consent prompt always names a boundary and never defaults to unlimited
37. executor aborts the step and the plan when revokerun fires
38. revokerun rolls back queued steps without executing them
39. revokerun writes a terminal immutablelog entry naming the user action
40. executor appends every step transition to the immutablelog
41. executor appends every grant, expiry and revoke event to the immutablelog
42. executor appends hash linked entries inside a single storage transaction
43. pagebridge masks typed values before they reach the log writer
44. pagebridge masks form state in observation payloads before logging
45. observation schema keeps field shapes but masks their values when policy says so
46. executor refuses to resume a session whose consentwindow has expired
47. executor renews a consentwindow only through a new explicit prompt
48. executor records allowlist misses as denied events without navigation
49. executor records originprofile misses as denied sensitive events
50. session start writes the consentscope grant into the immutablelog
51. session close seals the immutablelog with a completion hash
52. background serves log reads only through the audit accessor
53. audit accessor verifies the chain before returning entries
54. audit accessor refuses reads of a chain with a broken link
55. audit kinds grow with grant, expiry, revoke, deny and seal events
56. memory stores originprofiles per origin with kind grants and denials
57. memory stores the allowlist of automation origins per profile
58. memory stores active consentwindows with expiry timestamps
59. memory stores revokerun events with the halted step ids
60. memory stores sealed immutablelogs with their final hash
61. memory stores mask rules for field shapes per origin
62. memory expires closed consentwindows past their boundary
63. memory keeps revocation history visible for later consent prompts
64. memory never stores unmasked typed values of sensitive fields
65. progress records denied steps with their deny reason
66. progress records revoked runs as halted with the revoked step
67. memory exports the verified log chain for audit
68. memory attaches provenance to every log entry with origin and step
69. memory scopes allowlists per profile workspace
70. memory adapter seam documents the future append only backend
71. options manage the per origin automation allowlist with add and remove
72. options manage originprofiles with per kind grants and denials
73. options explain the denydefault posture in place beside its toggle
74. sidepanel shows the remaining consentwindow time per active run
75. sidepanel offers revoke consent at any point during a run
76. sidepanel confirm dialog names the halted steps on revokerun
77. popup shows the origin allowlist state of the active tab
78. popup requests an allowlist grant when a task targets a new origin
79. popup shows the originprofile summary of the active tab
80. sidepanel renders sensitive steps with their class badge
81. sidepanel shows masked values as redacted fields in step details

84. sidepanel shows the log chain verification status per run
85. sidepanel shows the seal hash at run completion
86. popup shows the denydefault notice for ungranted origins
87. sidepanel surfaces consentwindow expiry with a renew action

## 1.1.62 security part two

This release finishes the security core started in 1.1.61 by protecting secrets, messages and money. A secretvault keeps passwords and tokens out of logs, redactshots masks sensitive capture regions, schemastrict and origincheck guard every inbound command, and confirmpay, confirmdelete and confirmcreds put a human in front of payments, deletions and credential use. phishguard watches for lookalike origins, safedefaults constrain first visits, and the transparencypage shows every grant with its permdiff history.

Changes:

1. types introduce secretvault records with scope, label and provenance
2. types introduce redactshots regions with geometry and reason
3. types introduce schemastrict errors with path and expected shape
4. types introduce origincheck verdicts for inbound messages
5. types introduce connectallow entries for external senders
6. types introduce ratelimit buckets per origin and per session
7. types introduce confirmpay, confirmdelete and confirmcreds gate descriptors
8. types introduce phishguard verdicts with distance score and matched origin
9. types introduce safedefaults as the initial originprofile for unknown sites
10. types introduce permdiff records between two permission versions
11. transparencypage becomes a declared surface in the manifest
12. policy requires schemastrict validation of every inbound command before dispatch
13. policy rejects commands with unknown fields under schemastrict
14. policy requires origincheck on every runtime message before handling
15. policy requires origincheck on every port connection before accept
16. policy drops messages from senders absent from the connectallow list
17. connectallow ships empty by default with user managed entries only
18. policy applies ratelimit buckets to automation commands per origin
19. policy defers commands that exceed their bucket until the window resets
20. ratelimit bounds are user choices with no hidden ceiling
21. policy routes payment class steps through the confirmpay gate
22. policy routes destructive delete steps through the confirmdelete gate
23. policy routes credential use steps through the confirmcreds gate
24. confirmpay shows the amount, payee origin and target element of the step
25. confirmdelete shows the target, scope and irreversibility of the step
26. confirmcreds shows the credential label without revealing its value
27. policy requires a distinct human action for each gated step with no batch approval
28. secretvault stores secrets through the browser vault with no plaintext in storage
29. policy refuses secrets in step options, variables and plan text
30. phishguard compares login targets against granted origins for lookalike distance
31. executor validates each command against its schemastrict definition before dispatch
32. executor returns schemastrict errors with path and expected shape
33. background runs origincheck in every onmessage listener
34. background runs origincheck in every onconnect handshake
35. background closes ports from senders outside connectallow
36. executor consults the ratelimit bucket before each command dispatch
37. executor records deferred commands as deferred events
38. executor pauses at confirmpay gates until a human resolves them
39. executor pauses at confirmdelete gates until a human resolves them
40. executor pauses at confirmcreds gates until a human resolves them
41. executor resolves gates only through explicit user action and never through timeout
42. credential steps read values from the secretvault at the last possible moment
43. typed credential values flow from the vault to the field with no logging
44. executor never writes passwords or tokens into logs, memory or exports
45. executor redacts screenshot regions marked sensitive before storage
46. redactshots applies to viewport, element and stitched captures alike
47. redactshots regions derive from field shapes and user drawn masks
48. policy marks extracted markup untrusted before any render
49. executor routes untrusted renders through the sandboxframe from 1.1.60
50. executor refuses to inject untrusted markup into the page context
51. executor runs phishguard before any credential step on a new origin
52. phishguard blocks the step when the lookalike distance crosses the user threshold
53. phishguard names the matched known origin in the deny event
54. executor applies safedefaults to any origin without an originprofile
55. safedefaults grant reads only and deny every sensitive class
56. memory stores secretvault metadata with labels and scopes and never values
57. memory stores connectallow entries with their senders
58. memory stores ratelimit bucket state per origin and session
59. memory stores gate resolution events with their human action provenance
60. memory stores redactshots regions per origin and page template
61. memory stores phishguard verdicts with distance scores
62. memory stores permdiff records for each installed version
63. memory stores safedefaults applications with their first seen origin
64. immutablelog records gate opens, gate resolutions and phishguard denies
65. immutablelog records schemastrict rejections with no payload echo
66. immutablelog records origincheck drops without handler execution
67. memory expires phishguard verdicts past their freshness window
68. memory scopes secretvault metadata per profile workspace
69. progress records gate waits beside step durations
70. memory serves transparency data in one read for the transparencypage
71. transparencypage lists every active grant with origin, scope and boundary
72. transparencypage lists every consentwindow ever granted with its expiry
73. transparencypage lists connectallow entries with their senders

77. options never display or export secret values

## 1.1.63 session interface

After the security core of 1.1.62, this release turns the session itself into the primary interface. A sessiongrid lists every live and saved run, historysearch reaches across sessions and notes, and emptystate and errorsurface surfaces guide the user when there is nothing or something broken to show. Behind them the agent gains sitenotes, a per task scratchpad, runsummary distillation, semanticrecall of past extractions, correctionmemory from plan edits, and consentmemory of every origin decision.

Changes:

1. types introduce sitenotes records per origin with title and body
2. types introduce scratchpad records per task with append only entries
3. types introduce runsummary records distilled from completed runs
4. types introduce semanticrecall queries with text and origin scope
5. types introduce correctionmemory entries linking a step to its correction

7. types introduce sessiongrid rows with session id, origin set, state and outcome
8. types introduce historysearch queries with text, origin and time range

10. types introduce a cancelrun action with a rollback option descriptor

13. policy scopes scratchpad access to the owning task session
14. policy scopes correctionmemory and consentmemory reads to planning and prompting
15. policy refuses semanticrecall across origins outside the run scope
16. runsummary generation runs as an offscreenworker task
17. runsummary length follows the user configured window with no fixed cap
18. policy keeps consentmemory advisory for prompts and never auto grants
19. policy stores consent denials with the same weight as grants
20. errorsurface payloads classify causes as page, network, policy or gate

## 1.1.64 interface surfaces part one

This release gives the interface from 1.1.63 its full surfaces. The popup grows into a command surface, the sidepanel becomes a workspace, the dashboardpage opens a full page view in a new tab, and the optionspage gathers every setting in one place. onboarding walks a first run, the commandpalette and taskinput accept fuzzy commands and natural language goals, and plancards, stepapprove, diffpreview, stepstimeline and logstream carry a plan from review to live execution.

## 1.1.66 ecosystem part one

This release opens the ecosystem layer on the interface foundation of 1.1.65. A flowlibrary lets users browse, install, fork and share workflow templates, syncbridge hooks move manifests between machines behind explicit opt in, and background runs keep workflows executing without an open surface. attentionfeed collects everything that needs a human, runreplay walks a sealed chain step by step for audit, and outputcompare sets two competing runs beside each other.

16. syncbridge moves manifests only and never secrets or logs

18. runreplay reads only the immutablelog and stored captures

## 1.1.67 ecosystem part two

This release takes the ecosystem from 1.1.66 out of the browser and into every runtime. planlint checks plan files against the same policy engine, flowrun executes plans from the terminal, and exporttools move runs, extractions and notes to disk. headlesslib drives a remote browser without any extension surface, and esm, cjs and umd builds with complete typedefs ship one core to node, bun and deno through neutral platformtargets and runtimeadapters.

15. planlint exits non zero on error diagnostics
16. flowrun requests pass through the same proposal validation as the extension
17. headlesslib sessions keep consent gates but route them to a callback
18. headlesslib exposes the gate callback as a programmatic consent provider
19. policy keeps denydefault active in headlesslib mode

21. exporttools refuse to export unmasked log values

24. runtimeadapters probe for dom, storage, network and worker capabilities
25. capability probes downgrade features instead of failing the runtime
