## 1.1.68 performance part one

With the library shipping on every runtime from 1.1.67, this release starts the performance work. lazymods keep heavy code out of the startup path, debouncedom and batchquery cut redundant dom work, incrsnapshot and selcache avoid recomputing what did not change, and virtlist keeps long surfaces smooth. streamparse handles pages too large for one pass, chunkextract slices big tables into resumable windows, and the worker pool from 1.1.60 absorbs the load.

Changes:

1. types introduce lazymods descriptors with module id and load reason
2. types introduce debouncedom profiles with event kinds and windows
3. types introduce batchquery plans grouping selectors per snapshot
4. types introduce incrsnapshot deltas with base ref and change set
5. types introduce selcache entries with selector, resolution and generation
6. types introduce virtlist windows with row range and height map
7. types introduce streamparse tokens for large page parsing
8. types introduce chunkextract cursors for big tables
9. types introduce perf records with duration, query counts and cache hits
10. policy keeps lazy loading transparent to capability checks
11. policy bounds debouncedom windows by user choice only
12. selcache generations advance on every dom mutation batch
13. incrsnapshot requires a base snapshot ref from the same run
14. streamparse chunks pass through the same schemastrict validation
15. chunkextract cursors carry the table fingerprint for resume
16. lazymods declare their capability requirements ahead of load
17. batchquery plans fold repeated selectors into one pass
18. observation schema gains a delta flag for incrsnapshot payloads
19. policy refuses selcache hits from a stale generation
20. progress records perf data beside step outcomes
21. module loader resolves lazymods on first use with a prewarm hook
22. heavy parsers load lazily and stay out of the startup path
23. capture, compare and export modules load lazily behind their commands
24. dom observer debounces rapid mutation bursts into one batch
25. debouncedom coalesces scroll, input and resize storms per window
26. snapshot builder batches dom queries into a single query pass
27. batchquery executes grouped selectors in one offscreenworker task
28. snapshot builder computes deltas against the previous snapshot
29. incrsnapshot emits only changed regions and their fingerprints
30. incrsnapshot keeps full snapshots on a user configured cadence
31. selector resolver caches resolutions per generation
32. selcache invalidates wholesale on navigation events
33. selcache invalidates selectively on matching mutation fingerprints
34. selector resolver revalidates cached hits before dispatch
35. long lists render through a virtualized window in every surface
36. virtlist measures row heights and keeps a height map
37. virtlist recycles row nodes during scroll
38. datagrid, logstream and stepstimeline adopt virtlist
39. remaining inline parsers move into the worker pool from 1.1.60
40. worker queue gains backpressure for parse tasks
41. streamparse tokenizes large pages in chunks inside a worker
42. streamparse yields observations progressively as chunks complete
43. streamparse bounds memory by never holding the full page text
44. chunkextract slices big tables into row windows
45. chunkextract emits each window as a partial extraction result
46. chunkextract resumes from its cursor after interruption
47. chunkextract merges windows into the datagrid incrementally
48. offscreen documents host streamparse and chunkextract tasks
49. background runs from 1.1.66 reuse the same worker queue
50. executor records query counts and cache hits per step
51. executor records parse durations per worker task
52. executor skips recomputation when the snapshot delta is empty
53. executor deduplicates identical snapshot requests within a run
54. executor defers heavy capture work behind a user priority choice
55. executor cancels abandoned parse tasks when a run halts
56. memory stores selcache generations per run
57. memory stores incrsnapshot base refs per run
58. memory stores chunkextract cursors for resume
59. memory stores perf records per step for profiling
60. memory stores lazymods load telemetry for startup analysis
61. memory prunes selcache entries at run end
62. memory prunes incrsnapshot bases at run end
63. memory keeps perf records for the user configured window
64. memory scopes caches per profile workspace
65. memory stores virtlist height maps per surface for reuse
66. progress deltas reuse the incrsnapshot change format
67. memory records worker queue depth over time for tuning
68. memory attaches provenance to every perf record
69. memory exports perf records as one audit bundle
70. memory adapter seam documents the future metrics backend
71. sidepanel shows cache hits and query counts in the run footer
72. stepstimeline shows parse durations on snapshot nodes
73. datagrid appends chunkextract windows as they arrive
74. datagrid shows a chunk cursor progress line
75. logstream renders through virtlist without frame drops
76. optionspage configures the incrsnapshot cadence

79. optionspage configures the lazymods prewarm set

82. dashboardpage renders a perf summary per recent run

## 1.1.69 performance part two

This release closes phase four by making the platform from 1.1.68 polite, measured and budgeted. backpressure, domainlimits, politedelay, adaptivepoll and requestcoalesce pace batch work against sites, while runbudget, budgetalerts, timeoutcancel and cadence keep a run inside the resources the user chose. durationmeter, selectorprofile, steptrace and slowmo expose where time goes, and efficientresume, navdedupe, sessionreuse, artifactcompress, logprune, startupmeter, coldstart, batteryaware, networkaware, readparallel and warmselectors remove the remaining waste.

Changes:

1. types introduce backpressure signals for batch run queues
2. types introduce domainlimits with per domain concurrency slots
3. types introduce politedelay profiles with jitter and per domain floors
4. types introduce adaptivepoll windows with floor, ceiling and growth factors
5. types introduce requestcoalesce keys for repeated queries
6. types introduce runbudget records with memory and step budget fields
7. types introduce tabsuspend states for long waits
8. types introduce runcache entries keyed by resource digest
9. types introduce stepprefetch hints derived from plan structure
10. types introduce durationmeter samples per step
11. types introduce selectorprofile stats per selector
12. types introduce steptrace spans with start, end and cause
13. types introduce budgetalerts thresholds with severity levels
14. types introduce timeoutcancel policies per step
15. types introduce efficientresume checkpoints with cursor and digest
16. types introduce navdedupe keys for planned navigations
17. types introduce sessionreuse grants for authenticated profiles
18. types introduce artifactcompress codecs for stored artifacts
19. types introduce logprune rules with age and size windows
20. types introduce startupmeter samples for cold start
21. types introduce batteryaware scheduling states
22. types introduce networkaware retry policies with failure kind backoff
23. types introduce readparallel groups of independent page reads
24. types introduce warmselectors carry over records across steps

33. domainlimits cap concurrent steps per domain at the user chosen value
34. domainlimits queue overflow steps per domain lane
35. politedelay spaces batch requests per domain with jitter
36. politedelay honors per domain floors from siteprofiles

40. requestcoalesce fans the single result out to every waiter
41. runbudget tracks memory pressure per run through worker telemetry

67. artifactcompress compresses captures and logs at rest

## 1.1.70 resilience

Phase five opens where 1.1.69 closed the execution environment work, turning the engine from a best effort loop into a survivable run manager. Runs gain a tracked state machine with offline queues, idempotent step identifiers, checkpoints, side effect rollback, zombie reaping, heartbeats and cancellation with rollback, so a service worker restart or a lost connection no longer loses work. Consent gates stay in force and every bound remains a user choice.

Changes:

1. types adds runstate values queued running paused awaitingapproval completed failed cancelled and rolledback
2. types adds runrecord interface with planid sessionid state createdat and updatedat
3. types adds checkpointrecord interface with runid stepid digest and createdat
4. types adds rollbackitem interface describing one compensating step of a failed run
5. types adds queuedtask interface holding an approved plan awaiting execution
6. types adds heartbeatrecord interface with runid beat and at
7. types adds runtransition type pairing from and to runstate values
8. auditkind grows with queue resume checkpoint rollback reap and cancel events
9. toolstep gains an idempotencykey field derived from plan and step identity
10. planprogress gains a runid field linking progress to its run record
11. observation gains a digest field for checkpoint comparison
12. response envelope gains runstate so callers see the live machine state
13. resilience module groups every run lifecycle logic in one file

15. advancerun moves a runrecord through one legal transition or throws
16. initialrun creates a runrecord in the queued state for an approved plan
17. stepkey derives a deterministic idempotencykey from planid stepid and kind
18. queuesize returns the count of tasks waiting in the offlinequeue
19. enqueueoffline appends an approved plan with a monotonic sequence number
20. dequeueoffline returns the next queuedtask in sequence order
21. replayqueue drains every queued task once connectivity returns
22. replayqueue marks tasks as failed when their plan expired offline
23. makecheckpoint captures completed step ids a page digest and a timestamp

25. checkpointisvalid verifies a checkpointrecord against the run and page digest
26. runheartbeat refreshes the heartbeatrecord of a running run
27. heartbeatisstale returns true when the last beat is older than the configured window
28. heartbeatwindow loads the user configured staleness window without a hard cap
29. zombiecheck lists running runs whose heartbeat is stale
30. reaprun transitions a zombie run to failed and records a reap audit event
31. sideeffects lists the mutating steps a run already executed
32. rollbackrun builds compensating steps for executed side effects
33. rollbackrun leaves read-only steps without compensations
34. cancelrun moves a run to cancelled and returns the rollback choice
35. cancelrollback pairs a cancellation with an optional rollback execution
36. runfail transitions a run to failed and freezes its progress snapshot
37. runcomplete transitions a run to completed only when iscomplete reports success
38. runfromplan rebuilds a runrecord after a service worker restart

## 1.1.71 state depth

Where 1.1.70 made runs survivable, this release makes their state observable and safe. Every run keeps its own urlhistory and timeline, memory items carry provenance and expiry, storage is encrypted at rest with quota awareness, tabs isolate their state, concurrent runs lock their sessions, and the whole memory surface exports for audit.

Changes:

1. types adds urlvisit interface with url title at and runid
2. types adds runtimelineevent interface pairing a timestamp with a step or audit event
3. types adds provenancerecord interface with origin runid stepid and capturedat
4. types adds expiryrule interface pairing a memory key pattern with a lifetime
5. types adds quotareport interface with usage bytes remaining and cleanup candidates
6. types adds tabstate interface mapping a tabid to isolated run state
7. types adds lockrecord interface with holder runid acquiredat and expiresat
8. types adds auditexportrecord interface bundling runs memory and provenance
9. types adds memoryitem interface wrapping a stored value with its provenancerecord
10. auditkind grows with lock expire purge export and visit events

16. urlhistory deduplicates consecutive visits to the same url
17. runtimeline merges step results audit events and url visits into one ordered stream
18. runtimeline buckets events by phase for the timeline view
19. tabisolate namespaces memory keys per tabid so runs never share state

21. sessionlock acquires a lockrecord for a run on a session

23. sessionlock releases the lock on completion failure and cancel

63. memory accessors getlock and setlock persist the lockrecord

## 1.1.72 multi agent part three

Building on the state depth of 1.1.71, the fleet layer arrives with control and accountability. Agents get names, per agent budgets and permission scopes, human escalation, agent to agent review requests, run replay for audit, competing output comparison, consensus voting, a global killswitch and single agent pause, all without weakening the consent gates.

## 1.1.73 multi agent part four

Following the fleet control of 1.1.72, this release scales coordination itself. Sub agents spawn on demand under depth limits, parallel results aggregate into one report, interleaved timelines show every agent action, lessons spread across agents, resources arbitrate under prioritized lanes, workers scale by site load and costs share across the fleet.

## 1.1.74 navigation intelligence

After the fleet work of 1.1.73, attention returns to the page and how the agent moves through it. Navigation gains predicted prefetching, origin preconnection, web app deep links, closed tab reopening, trail restoration, consent aware pausing, plan intent detection, per domain rate limiting, clipboard opening, url safety verification and curated batch opening.

Changes:

1. types adds prefetchplan interface with predicted urls and confidence
2. types adds preconnecttarget interface with origin and expectedat
3. types adds deeplinkpattern interface matching a web app route
4. types adds closedtabrecord interface with url title and closedat
5. types adds navtrailentry interface with url stepid and at
6. types adds navintentrecord interface with predicted next urls
7. types adds ratelimitwindow interface with domain count and resetat
8. types adds safetyverdict interface with risk reasons and url
9. types adds linkbatch interface with curated urls and origin grants
10. types adds navpause interface marking a navigation paused for consent
11. auditkind grows with prefetch preconnect reopen safecheck and batch events
12. toolstep gains an intenthint field for predicted navigation
13. navigation module groups prefetch preconnect deep link and safety logics

16. prefetchpage warms predicted urls through speculative dns

18. prefetchpage drops predictions when the plan changes
19. preconnectorigin opens sockets to expected origins ahead of steps
20. preconnectorigin honors the host grants of the session

33. openclipboardurl requires the origin grant before opening

35. checksafeurl refuses hosts that imitate granted origins

## 1.1.75 data pipelines

With the navigation intelligence of 1.1.74 in place, extraction grows into full data pipelines. Large extracts stream to disk with resume, transforms reshape values between extraction and export, rows deduplicate and carry timestamps and source stamps, grids preview before export and a provlog records provenance for audit.

## 1.1.76 web api coverage

On top of the pipelines of 1.1.75, the transport layer widens to the web api surface. Server sent events, long polling, graphql subscriptions, multipart uploads and form posts join the vocabulary, while correlation ids, rate limit respect and per run response caching keep every call accounted.

## 1.1.77 vision and ocr

Where 1.1.76 connected the transports, this release teaches the agent to see. Image, region, pdf and video frame ocr join vision model descriptions, element crops, sensitive region redaction, screenshot grounding and dom pairing so every visual claim traces back to pixels.

## 1.1.79 data minimization

The chain from 1.1.78 closes phase five with the smallest possible data footprint. Page data stays local first, telemetry stays off by default, sync is opt in and encrypted when enabled, purge and export answer on request, cookie jars isolate tasks, artifacts clean up after runs and downloads wait in quarantine.

Changes:

1. types adds datainventory interface listing keys classes and sizes
2. types adds purgepolicy interface with scope and confirmation
3. types adds syncsettings interface with enabled classes and cadence

6. types adds quarantineentry interface with path status and verdict
7. types adds cleanupschedule interface with artifact classes and timing
8. types adds localrule interface marking fields that must not leave the device
9. types adds telemetrypolicy interface fixed to off by default
10. auditkind grows with purge sync quarantine and cleanup events
11. agentsession gains a jarid field for cookie isolation
12. runrecord gains an artifacts list for cleanup tracking
13. minimization module groups local first sync purge and quarantine logics
14. localfirst processes page data inside the extension before any export

## 1.1.80 cli expansion

The resilience and multi agent scale work of 1.1.79 left the engine powerful, and 1.1.80 turns the cli into a complete operator surface beside it. Deep manifest checks, the planlint command, the runworkflow command, the exportdata command and the headlessmode library runtime let users lint, run, export and replay without opening the browser ui. Every command reuses the same policy, protocol, memory and progress modules as the extension, so the consent gates hold everywhere.

Changes:

1. cli manifest command verifies every manifest key against the runtime policy allowlist
2. cli manifest command reports the source file and line of every permission declaration
3. cli manifest command validates content security policy hashes for every bundled script
4. cli manifest command validates web accessible resources against the reviewed resource set
5. cli manifest command refuses permission entries duplicated across the required and optional sets
6. cli manifest command checks the minimum chrome version against the apis recorded in the capability report
7. cli manifest command validates icon dimensions and file presence for every declared icon
8. cli gains the planlint command for static validation of saved plan files
9. planlint verifies that every step carries a reviewed action kind from the kind catalog
10. planlint verifies every selector against the selector grammar shared with policy
11. planlint reports steps whose options payload misses required fields for their kind
12. planlint flags steps whose target origin is absent from the consent allowlist
13. planlint computes a risk summary per plan with counts by risk class
14. planlint exits nonzero when a plan contains forbidden kinds
15. planlint reads a plan path argument or the default plan location from configuration
16. planlint prints findings in a stable line format consumable by editors
17. cli gains the runworkflow command to execute saved workflows from the terminal
18. runworkflow loads a workflow file and replays its steps through the library engine
19. runworkflow streams progress events to the terminal as each step completes
20. runworkflow honors the review gate by pausing for approval before sensitive steps
21. runworkflow writes an audit trail file beside the workflow after each run
22. runworkflow supports the dry run mode without side effects
23. runworkflow resumes execution from a checkpoint id
24. runworkflow reports a structured outcome summary with duration per step
25. runworkflow maps exit codes to failure classes documented in the readme
26. cli gains the exportdata command for session, audit and extraction data
27. exportdata writes the session history as json
28. exportdata writes the audit trail as json lines
29. exportdata writes extracted table data as csv
30. exportdata writes extracted data as markdown tables
31. exportdata selects a time window through from and to flags
32. exportdata writes to stdout when no output path is given
33. exportdata refuses to include secret material from the secret store
34. cli gains headlessmode as a library runtime without a browser extension
35. headlessmode runs the plan engine against recorded page state fixtures
36. headlessmode executes read only kinds against fixtures without a live tab
37. headlessmode reports unsupported kinds when a fixture cannot satisfy them
38. headlessmode loads fixtures from the directory named in configuration
39. headlessmode keeps the consent gates by requiring fixture scoped grants
40. headlessmode records outcomes with the same progress model as live runs
41. library index gains an entry point that accepts a plan, a state source and a policy
42. the library entry point returns a run handle with progress, pause and cancel
43. cli parses global configuration and verbosity flags before dispatching commands
44. cli help lists every command with a one line description
45. cli help prints the version banner from package.json
46. cli returns structured json output when the json flag is set
47. planlint shares the kind catalog with policy instead of keeping a copy
48. runworkflow shares the step executor with the background service worker
49. exportdata shares serializers with the extension export menu
50. headlessmode shares the observation schema with the live snapshot pipeline

52. build emits the headless library entry as its own dist target
53. build keeps every bundle free of underscored identifiers through the naming check

## 1.1.81 library modes

The cli expansion of 1.1.80 exposed one library entry point, and 1.1.81 makes every consumption mode first class. cjsbuild, umdbuild and typedecls join the esm build, neutraltarget keeps the platform surface behind one adapter seam, and bunruntime plus denoruntime join node as verified runtimes. package.json grows export conditions for every mode so the same consent-first library loads from any host.

## 1.1.82 site integration

The multi runtime library of 1.1.81 now meets the web. 1.1.82 defines the servercontract spoken between the static devthink.pro site and the extension, ships the staticdeploy configuration for a plain static host, and connects the chatbridge conversation surface to the browser through open sockets and sharedauth pairing. No platform functions are involved anywhere, and the relay server url is always a user setting, never a hardcoded default.

Changes:

1. servercontract module defines the message envelope between site and extension
2. servercontract carries a version field with capability negotiation
3. servercontract defines the sessioncreate, sessionjoin, eventpost and eventstream operations
4. servercontract defines event types for chat, plan proposals, plan review and progress
5. servercontract rejects envelopes that fail schema validation
6. servercontract keeps a stable operation id per message for correlation
7. socketrelay client connects to the user configured relay url over wss
8. socketrelay reconnects with exponential backoff
9. socketrelay multiplexes chat, review and progress streams over one socket
10. socketrelay authenticates every frame with the sharedauth session token
11. serverurl setting stores the relay url in storage.local with no default value
12. serverurl empty value disables the bridge completely
13. serverurl accepts any wss url the user chooses without vendor assumptions
14. serverurl is validated for scheme, host and port shape before saving
15. the extension ships no hardcoded relay url or vendor endpoint anywhere
16. chatbridge page widget renders the conversation on the static site
17. chatbridge sends task text to the extension through the relay
18. chatbridge renders proposed plans as review cards
19. chatbridge returns review decisions to the extension review gate
20. chatbridge never executes actions, the extension stays the only executor
21. chatbridge approves sensitive steps only through the extension approval flow
22. sharedauth mints pairing codes inside the extension options
23. sharedauth shows the pairing code with an expiry countdown
24. sharedauth exchanges the pairing code for a session token
25. sharedauth rotates the session token on every reconnect
26. sharedauth stores tokens scoped to the relay origin
27. sharedauth revokes all sessions with one click in the options
28. staticdeploy directory holds the devthink.pro static site assets
29. staticdeploy builds to hashed assets with an immutable cache header configuration
30. staticdeploy config targets a plain static host with zero server functions
31. staticdeploy config includes no edge functions, no redirect rules and no vendor runtime
32. the site works fully from static assets when opened from a local file or any static host
33. site manifest declares the servercontract version it speaks
34. the site asks the visitor for the relay url before connecting
35. the site stores the relay url in browser localstorage
36. origin checks validate the sender of every incoming extension message
37. external connect allowlist restricts which pages may talk to the extension
38. rate limiting caps bridge messages per session per second
39. audit trail records every bridge event with session id and operation
40. data minimization sends only plan text and statuses over the bridge
41. page content never crosses the bridge without an explicit consent flag
42. the site and the bridge collect no telemetry by default
43. bridge kill switch disables the socket and pairing instantly
44. popup bridge status icon shows connected, paired and offline states
45. offline queue buffers review requests while the socket is down
46. queued review requests replay with deduplication on reconnect
47. heartbeat frames keep the socket alive during long runs
48. relay sessions expire after a configurable idle window
49. relay allows one extension and one site member per session
50. session tokens never appear in urls or logs
51. the site and the extension negotiate features through the servercontract handshake
52. bridge consent gate asks the user before the first socket connection
53. tests/build.mjs gains the staticdeploy build step for the site assets
54. build emits the site to dist/site with hashed file names
55. verify workflow builds the site and asserts it contains only static file types
56. static site scan test refuses any serverless function file in the repository
57. source scan test refuses any hardcoded relay or vendor url in the code
58. release workflow packages the site as a static zip release asset
59. release workflow publishes the site zip beside the extension artifacts
60. package.json exports map stays free of site entries, the site ships as its own artifact
61. testrelay helper implements the servercontract for tests only
62. testrelay binds to localhost with a random free port
63. containerfile serves the static site for local bridge testing

66. verify workflow runs the bridge e2e test against the testrelay on localhost

## 1.1.83 gateway integration

The site bridge of 1.1.82 made devthink reachable from the open web, and 1.1.83 makes it reachable from any model provider. The openaicompat, anthropicgateway, geminigateway and ollamalocal adapters speak to endpoints the user configures through baseurlconfig, keys live in the keyvault, modellist discovers what a provider offers, and capabilityad advertises the tool catalog in return. Providers stay optional and local first, with budget tracking and guardrails on every parsed response.

Changes:

1. gateway module defines the provider adapter interface
2. gateway adapters share one request, stream and cancel contract
3. openaicompat adapter speaks the chat completions api shape
4. openaicompat adapter works with any compatible endpoint the user configures
5. openaicompat adapter streams response deltas as they arrive
6. anthropicgateway adapter speaks the messages api shape
7. anthropicgateway adapter streams content blocks
8. anthropicgateway adapter maps system prompts to the provider format
9. geminigateway adapter speaks the generate content api shape
10. geminigateway adapter streams candidate parts
11. geminigateway adapter maps tool schemas to the provider format
12. ollamalocal adapter speaks to a localhost endpoint
13. ollamalocal adapter defaults to localhost and never to a cloud url
14. ollamalocal adapter lists locally installed models
15. baseurlconfig stores one endpoint url per provider
16. baseurlconfig ships no default cloud endpoint for any provider
17. baseurlconfig validates scheme, host and path shape before saving
18. baseurlconfig allows per provider path prefixes for gateway deployments
19. keyvault stores api keys per provider with explicit consent
20. keyvault keeps keys in storage.local and never in sync storage
21. keyvault masks keys in every log, audit entry and error message
22. keyvault never includes keys in exportdata output
23. keyvault revokes keys with one click per provider
24. modellist fetches the available models from the active provider
25. modellist caches the model list with a configurable refresh window
26. modellist annotates models with context and modality hints when offered
27. capabilityad publishes the devthink tool catalog to the gateway in requests
28. capabilityad announces tool schemas in the provider specific format
29. capabilityad declares the consent requirement of every tool in its metadata
30. capabilityad advertises the plan review gate as a required capability

32. gateway routing falls back to the local provider when remote calls are not allowed
33. token budget tracking accounts input and output tokens per call

37. guardrail parser validates every model response against the plan schema

39. guardrail parser caps retry attempts at a user configured number

## 1.1.84 mcp server mode

The gateway adapters of 1.1.83 let devthink call any model, and 1.1.84 lets any agent client call devthink. mcpmode exposes the engine as a model context protocol server with the toolcatalog, resourceexpose and promptexpose surfaces over stdioserve and httpserve transports. Consent stays in the loop: sensitive tools wait for the human approval gate and every call lands in the audit trail.

Changes:

1. mcpmode entry point runs devthink as a model context protocol server
2. mcpmode initializes with capability negotiation and server metadata

## 1.1.85 native host bridge

The mcp server of 1.1.84 opened devthink to agent clients over the network, and 1.1.85 adds the optional native transport mined from the packages with native host bridges. An opt in nativehost manifest, the wsbridge localhost relay and the companionbin recipe extend reach to desktop processes while the manifest validator keeps the native messaging permission optional only. Nothing native runs until the user installs it, and a kill switch plus the audit trail cover every call.

Changes:

1. manifest declares the native messaging permission in the optional set only
2. cli manifest command allows the native messaging permission in the optional set and refuses it in the required set
3. nativehost module implements the runtime native messaging port
4. nativehost manifest template carries a generated extension id placeholder
5. nativehost installer writes the host manifest into the user profile directory
6. nativehost installer refuses system wide installs without an explicit flag
7. nativehost uninstaller removes the host manifest and its preferences
8. install consent gate explains the scope before any host registration
9. companionbin recipe builds the optional native companion process
10. companionbin speaks length prefixed json over stdin and stdout
11. companionbin handshake reports build and protocol version
12. wsbridge translates the native port to a localhost websocket server
13. wsbridge binds to localhost on a random free port
14. wsbridge authenticates frames with a per session token
15. wsbridge advertises the port and token to the extension over the native port
16. wsbridge expires sessions after an idle window
17. capability negotiation lists the host features on connect
18. native transport consent gate asks per call class
19. sensitive native calls route through the human approval gate
20. audit trail records every native call with class and outcome
21. native transport kill switch stops the host instantly
22. deny by default posture keeps the bridge disabled until installed
23. origin and session checks validate every incoming native frame
24. native messages carry correlation ids across a run
25. heartbeat frames detect host process liveness
26. graceful degradation keeps runs alive when the host is absent or outdated
27. structured errors map native failures to retry hints
28. companionbin sources live in the companion directory with a build script
29. companionbin build recipe uses plain node with no native compiler
30. companionbin ships as source plus a build command, never as a binary blob
31. nativehost settings panel shows install state and version
32. nativehost settings panel offers install, uninstall and diagnostics
33. diagnostics command reports port state, versions and last errors
34. native transport rate limiting caps calls per session
35. wsbridge supports one extension connection at a time
36. wsbridge refuses connections from other machines
37. companionbin exposes os dialog and notification surfaces behind consent
38. native surfaces enumerate through the capability negotiation
39. native surfaces refuse execution without an active consent grant
40. native surface results return to the plan engine as structured details
41. native transport events stream to subscribed mcp clients
42. nativehost reuses the servercontract envelopes for frames

44. the native host never receives key vault material

46. the extension survives a host crash and reattaches on restart

## 1.1.86 browser coverage

The native bridge of 1.1.85 removed the chromium only assumption in transport, and 1.1.86 removes it in packaging. firefoxprep and xpipack produce a firefox ready build, safariskeleton lays out the safari wrapper, and apimap plus the cross browser polyfills keep one codebase speaking every webextension dialect. The kind catalog, the policy gates and the audit format stay identical on every browser.

Changes:

1. apimap module records every webextension api the codebase touches

11. firefoxprep rewrites optional permission names where they differ

14. sidepanel polyfill opens a popup window when the sidepanel api is missing
15. sidepanel polyfill preserves the review gate layout inside the popup window
16. scripting polyfill falls back to tabs execute script on older engines
17. storage polyfill normalizes local storage behavior across browsers
18. storage polyfill keeps session tokens under the same keys on every browser
19. tabs polyfill normalizes tab query, create and update shapes
20. windows polyfill normalizes window creation bounds handling
21. notifications polyfill normalizes notification options per browser

## 1.1.87 publishing pipeline

Browser coverage in 1.1.86 multiplied the buildable artifacts, and 1.1.87 multiplies the publishing channels around them. nugetpack and mavenpack grow richer packages, containerpack ships a leaner image with the relay, vsixpack brings devthink into vs code, and the release workflow publishes a package for every mode with checksums, an sbom and attestations. One version sync step keeps every manifest on the same number.

## 1.1.88 documentation

The publishing pipeline of 1.1.87 spread devthink across many channels, and 1.1.88 makes every channel teachable. The userguide, the generated apiref and kindcatalog, the tutorials, the recipes and the walkthrough texts turn the accumulated capability set into a documented product. Generated documents compile from the sources in the verify workflow so the docs can never drift from the code.

## 1.1.89 quality gates

The documentation of 1.1.88 made the platform teachable, and 1.1.89 makes it provable. The e2esuite drives real browsers through consent, review and audit journeys, deepsmoke extends the chromium smoke with soak runs and restart recovery, perfbudget enforces startup, latency and memory ceilings grounded in the performance pool, and a11ycheck plus localeverify close the interface gaps. Every budget becomes a gate in the verify workflow, not a suggestion.

Changes:

1. e2esuite drives chromium through install, first run and consent
2. e2esuite covers the full plan proposal, review and execution journey
3. e2esuite covers sensitive step approval and rejection paths
4. e2esuite covers session pause, resume and cancel
5. e2esuite covers the popup, sidepanel and options surfaces
6. e2esuite covers cli manifest, planlint and runworkflow dry runs

## 1.1.90 feature audit

This release closes the phase six integration work and opens the consolidation chain that carries the library to 2.0.0. The poolaudit script cross references all 626 pool items in docs/12.crxfeaturemining.md against the code, the roadmap and the docs, so every item ends the release as implemented, planned or refused with a written reason. The gapclose changes repair the omissions the audit finds and docs/poolcoverage.md becomes the compliance baseline for the release candidates.

## 1.1.91 api freeze

The audit of 1.1.90 proved the feature surface complete, so this release freezes the external contracts before the release candidates. protocolv2 pins the message schemas, every surface publishes a capmanifest, and the deprecation policy defines what stability means until 2.0.0. No new capability enters the library, only versioned contracts, manifests and guarantees.

Changes:

1. tests/apifreeze.mjs enumerates every message type declared in protocol.ts
2. apifreeze verifies every message type carries the versioned envelope
3. apifreeze verifies the protocol version constant matches version.ts
4. apifreeze verifies the frozen tool catalog covers every action kind in policy.ts
5. apifreeze verifies the observation schema keeps its version field forward compatible
6. apifreeze verifies the response envelope covers success, error and cancel outcomes
7. apifreeze verifies every message field has a documented type in the schemas
8. apifreeze verifies the cli surface matches the library exports in index.ts
9. apifreeze verifies the mcp tool surface matches the frozen tool catalog
10. apifreeze verifies the sidepanel message surface matches the background handlers
11. apifreeze verifies the popup message surface matches the background handlers
12. apifreeze verifies the pagebridge message surface matches the injected bridge
13. apifreeze verifies the library surface exposes no undeclared symbols
14. apifreeze verifies every manifest permission maps to a consuming capability
15. apifreeze hashes every frozen schema into tests/artifacts/apifreeze.json
16. apifreeze exits nonzero when a frozen schema hash changes without a version bump

18. apifreeze joins the verify workflow as a required check

24. docs/schemas/capability.schema.json freezes the capability report
25. docs/schemas/audit.schema.json freezes the audit event shape
26. docs/schemas/memory.schema.json freezes the memory record shapes
27. docs/schemas/progress.schema.json freezes the progress event shapes
28. docs/schemas/tool.schema.json freezes the tool catalog entry shape
29. protocolv2 sets the protocol major version to two across protocol.ts and version.ts

31. protocol negotiation accepts version one messages with a deprecation notice
32. protocol negotiation refuses versions above two until a future major bump
33. every frozen schema carries the protocolv2 id and a frozen semantic version

The api freeze is the candidate gate of the chain: from this release on every later release runs the apifreeze gate, verifies the frozen schemas stayed hash stable since 1.1.91 and records the release candidate readiness against the pinned contracts — the stability rules of docs/stability.md hold until 2.0.0 opens the next major line.

## 1.1.92 migration tools

The frozen contracts of 1.1.91 need a bridge for existing automation assets, so this release builds the migration tooling. The migrateplan command converts version one plans into protocolv2 plans, importautoma brings automa workflows into the reviewed plan format, and further importers cover selenium ide, ui vision and tabular sources. Every importer converts first and never executes anything outside the normal consent gates.

## 1.1.93 performance hardening

After the migration bridge of 1.1.92 the consolidation turns to measured performance. coldstart cuts the service worker and surface startup cost, membudget enforces a per run memory ceiling the user chooses, batteryaware and netaware adapt scheduling to the device, and parallelreads completes the parallel read path. Every budget is measured and written into docs/perfbudgets.md.

Changes:

1. tests/coldstart.mjs measures service worker startup across repeated loads
2. coldstart records the startup breakdown per module into tests/artifacts/coldstart.json
3. coldstart compares every measurement against the documented budget
4. coldstart fails the run when the startup budget is exceeded
5. tests/membudget.mjs measures the peak memory of a full run against a fake tab
6. membudget measures the observation memory of a large page fixture
7. membudget measures the capture store growth across a long run
8. membudget compares every measurement against the documented budget
9. the performance suite measures the per step duration of every action kind
10. the performance suite measures selector resolution cost across steps
11. the performance suite measures snapshot cost on pages with many elements
12. the performance suite measures the export cost of large extracts
13. the performance suite measures the local protocol round trip latency
14. the performance suite records a baseline artifact per release
15. the baseline diff flags regressions beyond the noise thresholds
16. battery status queries run only when the platform api is available
17. network information queries run only when the platform api is available
18. coldstart defers the capture module load until first use
19. coldstart defers the debugger module load until attach
20. coldstart defers the importer modules until the import command runs
21. coldstart defers the dashboard module until the dashboard opens
22. coldstart trims the background startup to the message router and the gates
23. coldstart lazy loads the vision helpers on the first capture request
24. coldstart lazy loads the export helpers on the first export request
25. coldstart removes the remaining top level await chain from the background
26. the popup renders its first frame before capability discovery completes
27. the sidepanel renders its first frame before history loading completes
28. module init cost is logged once per startup for diagnosis
29. membudget enforces a per run memory ceiling read from configuration
30. membudget defaults to unlimited until the user sets a value
31. membudget tracks the live observation size during runs
32. membudget tracks the capture store size during runs
33. membudget tracks the session history size during runs
34. membudget raises budget alerts before the ceiling is reached
35. membudget pauses the run for review when the ceiling is reached
36. membudget never kills a run without an explicit user choice
37. membudget prunes thumbnails first when trimming the capture store
38. membudget compresses stored artifacts when the budget tightens
39. membudget reports the per component memory share in the run report
40. snapshot cadence adapts to the memory pressure signal
41. large page reads stream to disk when the observation exceeds the threshold
42. the run report includes the peak memory and the budget status
43. batteryaware reads the battery level through the battery status api when present
44. batteryaware defers non critical runs while the battery is low
45. batteryaware lets the user disable the deferral by configuration
46. batteryaware surfaces the deferral reason in the run queue
47. batteryaware resumes deferred runs when the battery recovers
48. batteryaware avoids heavy capture work in battery saver mode
49. netaware reads the connection type through the network information api when present
50. netaware schedules batch work only on unmetered connections by user choice
51. netaware applies the network aware retry policy on flaky connections
52. netaware reduces parallelism on slow connections
53. netaware reports the applied network profile in the run report
54. netaware lets the user pin a network profile by configuration
55. parallelreads completes the parallel read path for multi tab extraction
56. parallelreads batches independent read steps into one execution wave
57. parallelreads joins the wave results into one observation set
58. parallelreads respects the per domain concurrency limit
59. parallelreads keeps selectors warm across the wave
60. parallelreads cancels the remaining reads when the run is cancelled
61. parallelreads reports per read timings in the step results
62. the extraction engine coalesces repeated queries across steps
63. the observation pipeline debounces rapid mutation bursts
64. the snapshot builder batches dom queries per frame
65. incremental snapshots replace full ones after the first observation
66. selector caching invalidates on dom mutations
67. the dashboard virtualizes long list rendering beyond a threshold
68. parsing work moves to a worker for large extracts
69. offscreen documents host background capture work when needed

## 1.1.94 security hardening

The performance budgets of 1.1.93 hold, so this release hardens the security surface for the release candidates. The pentest checklist runs end to end against a local profile, cspaudit verifies every injected surface, permdiff proves the permission set never grew silently, and the transparency page reaches completion. The security review doc closes the release with the findings and their fixes.

## 1.1.95 multi agent certification

The scenario docs describe the certified topologies and their consent model.

The sweep script replays every recorded failure, matrixverify runs the full surface matrix across kinds, surfaces and transports, and the telemetry free verification proves no request leaves the machine without consent. ## 2.0.0-rc.2 release candidate two

The first candidate of 1.1.98 passed its matrix, so the second candidate polishes what remains and prepares the go decision. The final polish clears the small defects, the changelog assembles the complete story, the migration guide covers every supported path, and the readiness review signs the 2.0.0 go decision with evidence. This is the last release before the platform version.

## 2.0.0 the agentic browser platform

The last release of the chain closes the arc that started at 1.1.31: after sixty eight releases the library is an agentic browser platform, with unlimited consent-first agent capability, the full action vocabulary, perception through snapshots and vision, the debugging timeline, the workflow engine, the agent protocol, multi agent coordination and every library mode from npm to container. Version 2.0.0 freezes, verifies and ships that platform. The schemas become the frozen contract, the final sweeps run across every domain, the documentation and the example gallery reach completion, and the release engineering publishes the stable artifacts on every channel. The closing audit then walks every roadmap item from 1.1.32 to 1.1.99 and records each one as shipped or consciously superseded with a recorded reason.

## Chain closure record of 2.0.0

The 2.0.0 platform release closed the chain this document records. The release candidate two section folded into the platform release without a separate candidate: the readiness gate (tests/readiness.mjs walking every release gate against the recorded evidence and answering the go decision of docs/readiness.md), the migration bridge (the migrateplan command with the version one, automa, selenium, ui vision and tabular importers and their fixtures), the example gallery (the thirty six recipes of tests/code/recipes with the runner of tests/recipes.mjs green in the verify lane, the cli recipes command and the sidepanel one click import) and the closing stamps (the platform changelog entry, the release notes, the roadmap progress line, the todo closure and the five package channels with the rubygems gem beside npm, maven, nuget and the container) all shipped inside 2.0.0. The deprecation window the 1.1.91 api freeze opened closed at this release exactly as docs/deprecation.md recorded: the protocol floor speaks major two only, the deprecated fields removed with both promised sunsets executed, and the version one assets ride the migration bridge. The closing audit walks every roadmap item from 1.1.32 to 1.1.99 with each item recorded as shipped or consciously superseded with a written reason in the audit trail of this document's progress line and the worklog the release lane maintains.

## Final polish closure record of 2.0.2

The 2.0.2 final polish release closed the gap set the closing verification dispatches recorded against this roadmap. The six audit dispatches of the closing verification (every release section from 1.1.32 to 2.0.0, the rc.2 fold, the 2.0.0 section and the drift records) found sixteen never-built items: the thirteen rc.2 final polish and migration items (the keyboard focus order, the high contrast theme, the notification plurals, the dashboard panel resize, the unused-style pruning, the icon set, the store screenshots, the version negotiation banner, the once-per-user migration prompt, the release provenance stamps, the migration guide faq, the migration guide rollback chapter and the progress replay provenance) and the three 2.0.0 items (the soak run, the wcag accessibility sweep and the web store package). Every one shipped at the source in 2.0.2: the focusorderof, highcontrasttokens, pluralize, panelwidths and virtlist families in the surface modules with the stylepruning enforcement test; the v1sunset and migrationprompt states with the once-per-session banner and the once-per-user prompt through the notification path; the provenancestampof family of progress.ts with the replay stamps; the soak gate of tests/soak.mjs and the wcag gate of tests/wcag.mjs with their artifacts and their releasegates rows; the icon family of icons.ts materialized into the archive the packageextension gate asserts; the web store listing draft of docs/webstore.md with the screenshot checklist and the operator-published submission posture the readiness review records; the migration guide chapters; and the documentation completion (docs/21.librarymodes.md, docs/24.gatewayguide.md, the emulation section of docs/05, the emulation row of docs/01, the stale artifact table refresh). The store submission itself stays operator published as the residual risks of docs/readiness.md record: the release workflow publishes the registry channels and the github release, never the store. The closing audit trail of this document, the changelog and the worklog carries the per-item evidence.
